# Revenue Code Extract — New Revenue Code of the City of Malabon 2016

Consolidated reference for BizTrack fee assessment and permit processing.

## Provenance

| Field | Value |
|---|---|
| Source instrument | New Revenue Code of the City of Malabon 2016, City Ordinance No. A10-2016, enacted 5 December 2016 |
| Physical source | Scanned copy, 223 PDF pages; **ordinance page = PDF page − 4** (page references below cite PDF pages unless marked "ord.") |
| Extraction method | Transcription from page images (`revcode/png/p-0NN.png`), cross-checked against OCR text; disputed figures re-verified at 2×–6× magnification. `[sic]` and `[?]` flags mark defects printed in the ordinance itself — nothing was silently corrected. All source defects are consolidated in Appendix A. |
| Extraction date | 2026-07-24 |
| Assembly | Concatenated from ten extract files (A1–A10), reorganized into ordinance order. Boundary overlaps between adjacent extracts were deduplicated — the fuller capture was kept and each drop is noted inline at the part banners. |

L stray-animal impounding and art. Q excavations captured only as incidental context/headers); Ch. IV sports-complex article (tail captured as context); Ch. V B/C/F detail (parking, cemetery, tourism); Ch. VI (city hospital/health-facility rates; Annexes J–K tail captured incidentally) |

Note on BFP/FSIC: this ordinance contains **no Bureau of Fire Protection fee schedule** — the Fire Safety Inspection Certificate fee is a national imposition (RA 9514, collected as a percentage of local fees). Ch. III art. O (flammables storage) is the ordinance's fire-adjacent data source.

# New Revenue Code of the City of Malabon 2016 (Ordinance A10-2016)
## Chapter II, Articles E–I: Printing & Publication, Franchise, Professional, Amusement, Delivery Truck/Van

# ARTICLE E. Tax on Printing and Publication (PDF p.34 / ord. p.30) — FULL

## Sec. 2E.01 — Imposition of Tax
- **applies_to**: business of persons engaged in the printing and/or publication of books, cards, posters, leaflets, handbills, certificates, receipts, pamphlets, and others of similar nature
- **basis**: percentage_of_gross (newly started: fraction of capital investment)
- **rule/schedule** (verbatim):
  > There is hereby levied a tax at the rate of seventy-five percent (75%) of one percent (1%) of the gross annual receipts for the preceding calendar year on the business of persons engaged in the printing and/or publication of books, cards, posters, leaflets, handbills, certificates, receipts, pamphlets, and others of similar nature.
  >
  > In the case of a newly started business, the tax shall be one-twentieth (1/20) of one percent (1%) of the capital investment. In the succeeding calendar year, regardless of when the business started to operate, the tax shall be based on the gross receipts for the preceding calendar year, or any fraction thereof, as provided herein.
- **conditions/notes**:
  - Effective rate: 0.75% of gross annual receipts of the preceding calendar year.
  - Newly started business: 1/20 of 1% = 0.05% of capital investment; from the succeeding calendar year onward, gross-receipts basis applies regardless of start date.
  - In-lieu-of relationship: printing/publication is a percentage-tax article separate from the Article J graduated business tax (LGC Sec. 143 pattern); this article is the operative levy for such businesses in the fee calculator. (The in-lieu-of framing is structural — the article itself does not restate it on these pages.)
- **unclear**: none.

## Sec. 2E.02 — Exemptions
- **rule** (verbatim):
  > The receipts from the printing and/or publishing of books or other reading materials prescribed by the Department of Education, Culture and Sports as school texts or references shall be exempt from the tax herein imposed.

## Sec. 2E.03 — Time of Payment
- **rule** (verbatim):
  > The tax imposed in this Article shall be due and payable in quarterly installments to the City Treasurer, within the first twenty (20) days following each quarter. In the case of a newly-started business, the tax shall be paid before the business starts to operate.
- **note**: On the page the sentence runs "…following each quarter In the case of…" — the period after "quarter" is faint/absent in print [p.34]; sense is unambiguous.

---

# ARTICLE F. Franchise Tax (PDF p.34–36 / ord. p.30–32) — FULL

## Sec. 2F.01 — Definition
- **rule** (verbatim):
  > When used in this Article, *franchise* is a right or privilege, affected with public interest which is conferred upon private persons or corporation, under such terms and conditions as the government and its political subdivision may impose in the interest of public welfare, security and safety.

## Sec. 2F.02 — Coverage
- **applies_to** (verbatim):
  > a) All business enjoying a franchise either granted by the Congress of the Philippines, national franchising offices, or the "Sangguniang Panlungsod" when operating within the territorial jurisdiction of Malabon City shall be subject to the levy and other City regulatory measures;
  >
  > b) All government-owned or -controlled corporations (GOCCs) engaged in public utility, public service and regulated amusements operation in Malabon City; and
  >
  > c) Any activity which the City government is authorized by law to provide, establish, maintain, operate or grant establishments in operation thereof through franchise to private persons such as, but not limited, to communicating and transportation terminals, stalled vehicles/towing and impounding services, toll roads, public corral, City pound, slaughterhouses, livestock markets, public markets, "talipapa", electric supply generation and distribution, public cemeteries, sewerage system, waste collection and disposal and other similar public utility.

## Sec. 2F.03 — Imposition of Tax
- **applies_to**: businesses enjoying a franchise (per 2F.02)
- **basis**: percentage_of_gross (newly started: fraction of capital investment)
- **rule/schedule** (verbatim, re-verified at 2x zoom):
  > There is hereby imposed a tax on business enjoying a franchise tax, at a rate of seventy-five percent (75%) of one percent (1%) of the gross annual receipts, which shall include both cash sales and sales on account realized during the preceding calendar year within this province, excluding the territorial limits of the city.
  >
  > In the case of a newly started business, the tax shall be one-twentieth (1/20) of one percent (1%) of the capital investment. In the succeeding calendar year, regardless of when the business started to operate, the tax shall be based on the gross receipts for the preceding calendar year, or any fraction thereof, as provided herein.
  >
  > The capital investment to be used as basis of the tax of a newly started business as herein provided shall be determined in the following manner.
  >
  > (a) If the principal office of the business is located in the city, the paid-up capital stated in the articles of corporation in case of corporations, or in any similar document in case of other types of business organization, shall be considered as capital investment.
  >
  > (b) In the case of a branch or sales office located in the city which commences business operations during the same year as the principal office but which is located in another locality, the paid-up capital referred in (a) shall mean the amount of the capital investment made for the said branch or sales office.
  >
  > (c) Where the newly started business is a branch or sales office commencing operations at a year later than that of the principal office, capital investment shall mean the total funds invested in the branch or sales office.
- **conditions/notes**:
  - Effective rate: 0.75% of gross annual receipts (preceding calendar year, cash sales + sales on account).
  - Newly started: 0.05% (1/20 of 1%) of capital investment, with the (a)/(b)/(c) branch/principal-office rules above; succeeding year switches to gross-receipts basis.
  - In-lieu-of relationship: franchise-tax article stands apart from the Article J graduated business tax (same structural note as Article E).
- **unclear**:
  - [?] p.35: the phrase "within **this province**, excluding the territorial limits of the city" is what the page literally prints (verified at 2x zoom). This is an apparent drafting carry-over from LGC Sec. 137 (the provincial franchise-tax provision); read literally it would exclude city receipts, which contradicts the article's purpose. Transcribed as printed; flag for legal review before hard-coding territorial scope.
  - [?] p.35: "There is hereby imposed a tax on business enjoying a franchise **tax**" — the trailing "tax" is on the page (apparent typo for "a franchise").

## Sec. 2F.04 — Exemptions
- **rule** (verbatim):
  > The term businesses enjoying franchise shall not include holders of certificates of public convenience for the operation of public vehicles for reason that such certificates are not considered as franchises.

## Sec. 2F.05 — Time of Payment
- **rule** (verbatim):
  > The tax imposed in this Article shall be due and payable in quarterly installments to the City Treasurer, within the first twenty (20) days following each quarter. In the case of a newly-started business, the tax shall be paid before the business starts to operate.
- **note**: same faint period after "quarter" as 2E.03 [p.36].

## Sec. 2F.06 — Administrative Provisions
- **rule** (verbatim):
  > Any franchise holder who intend to operate in Malabon City shall file an application with the Office of the City Mayor and/or the City Secretary together with the following:
  >
  > a) Copy of the franchise
  > b) Certificate of registration issued by the Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC)
  > c) Current year plan of operation in the City
  > d) Last year's gross receipts from operation in the City, if applicable.

# ARTICLE G. Professional Tax (PDF p.36–38 / ord. p.32–34) — CONCISE (adjacent, out of workflow)

## Sec. 2G.01 — Imposition of Tax
- **applies_to**: each person engaged in the exercise or practice of his profession requiring government examination
- **basis**: fixed
- **rate** (verbatim): "an annual professional tax … at the rate of Three hundred pesos (P300.00)"

## Sec. 2G.02 — Coverage
- Professionals who passed the bar examinations, or any board or other examinations conducted by the Professional Regulation Commission (PRC).

## Sec. 2G.03 — Exemption
- Professionals exclusively employed in the government are exempt.

## Sec. 2G.04 — Payment of the Tax
- Must be paid before the profession can be lawfully pursued; a line of profession does not become exempt even if conducted with some other profession for which the tax has been paid.

## Sec. 2G.05 — Time of Payment
- Payable annually, on or before 31 January of each year, to the City Treasurer. A person first beginning to practice after January must pay the full tax before engaging therein.

## Sec. 2G.06 — Place of Payment
- Paid to the city where the professional maintains his principal office if practicing in several places. [Text on p.36–37 reads "…legally authorized to practice his profession in this province shall pay to the city where he maintains his principal office…" — "this province" as printed.]

## Sec. 2G.07 — Administrative Provisions (summary)
- (a) Payment entitles practice anywhere in the Philippines without other national/local tax or fee for the practice; (b) Treasurer requires presentation of valid PRC professional license before accepting payment; (c) employers must require proof of payment before employment and annually thereafter; (d) professionals must write the official receipt number on deeds, receipts, prescriptions, reports, books of account, plans and designs, surveys and maps.

## Sec. 2G.08 — Requirements (summary)
- (a) employers within Malabon City require payment before employment and annually thereafter; (b) professionals write receipt number, date and place of issue on professional documents; (c) every individual, association, partnership and corporation in the city submits a certified list of professionals in their employ to the City Treasurer on or before the last day of March each year, listing: 1) name, 2) profession, 3) amount of tax paid, 4) date and number of official receipts, 5) year covered, 6) place of payment.

---

# ARTICLE H. Amusement Tax on Admission (PDF p.38–39 / ord. p.34–35) — CONCISE (adjacent, out of workflow)

## Sec. 2H.01 — Definitions
- (a) Amusement: pleasurable diversion and entertainment; synonymous to relaxation, avocation, pastime or fun. (b) Amusement Places: theaters, cinemas, concert halls, circuses, and other places of amusement where one seeks admission to entertain oneself by viewing the show or performances.

## Sec. 2H.02 — Imposition of Tax
- **applies_to**: proprietors, lessees, or operators of theaters, cinemas, concert halls, circuses, boxing stadia, and other places of amusement
- **basis**: percentage_of_gross (admission fees)
- **rate** (verbatim):
  > …at the rate of ten percent (10%) of the gross receipts from admission fees. For this purpose, proprietors, lessees or operator of cinema who exhibits or show locally produced films shall be at the rate of ten percent (10%) of gross receipts from admission fees. (As per Republic Act No. 9640)
- **note**: both the general rate and the locally-produced-films rate print as 10% — verified on p.38 image (RA 9640 capped the amusement tax at 10%).

## Sec. 2H.03 — Manner of Computing Tax
- For theaters/cinemas, the tax is first deducted and withheld by proprietors/lessees/operators and paid to the City Treasurer before gross receipts are divided with film distributors.

## Sec. 2H.04 — Exemption
- Operas, concerts, dramas, recitals, painting and art exhibitions, flower shows, musical programs, literary and oratorical presentations are exempt — EXCEPT pop, rock or similar concerts.

## Sec. 2H.05 — Time and Place of Payment
- Due and payable by the proprietor/lessee/operator within the first twenty (20) days of the month next following that for which they are due, to the City Treasurer, before gross receipts are divided with distributors. Itinerant operators: tax paid immediately after the last full show or performance.

## Sec. 2H.06 — Administrative Provisions (summary)
- (a) Filing of Return: sworn monthly return of gross admission receipts attached upon payment; (b) Forms of Tickets: serially numbered two-part tickets (name of place + admission price; serial on both halves; one half dropped in a locked box opened only before a City Treasurer representative); (c) Registration of Tickets: all admission tickets must be registered with and stamped by the Office of the City Treasurer; unregistered tickets on premises unlawful; (d) Verification of Tickets: Mayor/Treasurer may post authorized inspectors at gates with access to all tickets from opening to closing time.

# ARTICLE I. Annual Fixed Tax for Every Delivery Truck or Van of Manufacturers or Producers, Wholesalers of, Dealers or Retailers in, Certain Products (PDF p.39–40 / ord. p.35–36) — FULL

## Sec. 2I.01 — Imposition of Tax
- **applies_to**: every truck, van or any motor vehicle used by manufacturers, producers, wholesalers, dealers or retailers in the delivery or distribution of any product for servicing business customers within Malabon City, to sales outlets or consumers, directly or indirectly, within the City
- **basis**: per_unit (annual fixed amount per vehicle)
- **rule/schedule** (verbatim, re-verified at 2x zoom across the p.39/p.40 page break):
  > There is hereby imposed an annual fixed tax for every truck, van or any motor vehicle used by manufacturers, producers, wholesalers, dealers or retailers in the delivery or distribution of any product for servicing business customers with Malabon City as may hereafter be determined by the Sangguniang Panlungsod, to sales outlets, or consumers, whether directly or indirectly, within the City in the amount of Seven Hundred Fifty Pesos (P750.00). For motorized or any other delivery vehicle used for such purpose, the tax shall be One Hundred Pesos (₱100.00) annually.

  | Vehicle | Annual fixed tax |
  |---|---|
  | Every delivery truck, van or any motor vehicle (manufacturers, producers, wholesalers, dealers, retailers) | P750.00 |
  | "Motorized or any other delivery vehicle used for such purpose" (as printed) | P100.00 |

- **unclear**:
  - [?] p.39: "for servicing business customers **with** Malabon City" — page prints "with" (verified at 2x zoom); almost certainly intends "within".
  - [?] p.40: the P100.00 tier prints "For **motorized** or any other delivery vehicle" (verified at 2x zoom). Given trucks/vans/motor vehicles are already taxed P750, this tier plausibly intends "non-motorized" (e.g., pedicabs, pushcarts); transcribed as printed — flag for legal confirmation before coding the P100 tier.

## Sec. 2I.02 — Exemption
- **rule** (verbatim):
  > The manufacturers, producers, wholesalers, dealers, and retailer referred to in the preceding sections shall be exempt from the payment of the peddler's tax in the sale of any merchandise or article of commerce imposable by the city.

## Sec. 2I.03 — Time and Place of Payment
- **rule** (verbatim):
  > The tax imposed in this Article shall accrue on the first day of January and shall be paid to the City Treasurer within the first twenty (20) days of January. In the case of newly-started business or newly-acquired motor vehicle, the owner or operator of the same shall pay the tax before the motor vehicle is allowed to operate in the City roads.

## Sec. 2I.04 — Administrative Provision
- **rule** (verbatim):
  > No motor vehicle used for the delivery of any products or service to customers shall be allowed to operate within the City of Malabon without a valid sticker issued by the Office of the City Mayor. Such sticker shall be issue after the owner of such motor vehicle shall have paid the corresponding fee for said sticker.
- **note**: "shall be issue" as printed [p.40] (sic, for "issued"). A separate sticker fee applies in addition to the tax.

# Article J. Graduated Tax on Business

## Sec. 2J.01 — Definitions (PDF pp. 40–45 / ord. pp. 36–41)
- **applies_to**: terms used in Article J
- **basis**: n/a (definitions)
- **schedule**: none. Terms defined, (a)–(aa):
  - (a) Advertising Agency
  - (b) Agricultural Products (incl. rule that produce remains "agricultural" whether raw or preserved through simple processes such as freezing, drying, salting, smoking, or stripping for purposes of preserving or otherwise preparing said products for the market)
  - (c) Amusement
  - (d) Amusement Places (theaters, cinemas, concert halls, circuses and other places of amusement where one seeks admission to entertain oneself by seeing or viewing the show or performance)
  - (e) Banks and other Financial Institutions (incl. non-bank financial intermediaries, lending investors, finance and investment companies, pawnshops, money shops, insurance companies, stock markets, stock brokers, and dealers in securities and foreign exchange, as defined under applicable law, or rules and regulations thereunder)
  - (f) Brewer (incl. all persons who manufacture fermented liquors of any description for sale or delivery in cases or other receptacles, except manufacturers of tuba, basi, tapuy or similar domestic fermented liquors, whose daily production does not exceed two hundred gauge liters)
  - (g) Business Agent (incl. all persons who act as agents of others in the transaction of business with any public officer, as well as those who conduct collecting, advertising, employment, or private detective agencies)
  - (h) Cabaret/Dance Hall (incl. any place or establishment where dancing is permitted to the public in consideration of any admission, entrance, or any other fee paid on, before or after the dancing, and where professional hostesses or dancers are employed)
  - (i) Capital Investment (is the capital that a person employs in any undertaking, or which he contributes to the capital of a partnership, corporation, or any other juridical entity or association)
  - (j) Corredera [as printed on p. 42; refers to livestock/animal dispatch — see unclear]
  - (k) Cockpit
  - (l) Contractor (long definition enumerating included businesses)
  - (m) Dealer
  - (n) [not separately legible as a lettered item; letters (m)–(q) run: Dealer, Franchise, Importer, Manufacturer — see unclear]
  - (o) Franchise
  - (p) Importer
  - (q) Manufacturer
  - (r) Marginal Farmer or Fisherman
  - (s) Motor Vehicle
  - (t) Peddler
  - (u) Public Market
  - (v) Rectifier
  - (w) Restaurant ("refers to any place which provides food to the public and accepts orders from them at a price. This term includes caterers.")
  - (x) Retail ("means a sale where the purchaser buys the commodity for his consumption, irrespective of the quantity of the commodity sold")
  - (y) Vessel
  - (z) Wharfage
  - (aa) Wholesale ("means a sale where the purchaser buys or imports the commodities for resale to persons other than the end user regardless of the quantity of the transaction")
- **conditions/notes**: Definitions are prose only; no rates. Full verbatim text of each definition is on PDF pp. 40–45.
- **unclear**: item (j) on PDF p. 42 — first word of the term (transcribed here as "Corredera") is stylized; the exact lettering of items (m)–(q) on PDF p. 43 was read from a lower-resolution pass; if definitions text is needed verbatim for the app, re-transcribe pp. 42–43 specifically.

## Sec. 2J.02 — Imposition of Tax (PDF pp. 45–54 / ord. pp. 41–50)
"There is hereby imposed on the following persons who establish, operate, conduct or maintain their respective business within the City a graduated business tax in the amounts hereafter prescribed:"

### (a) Manufacturers, assemblers, importers, repackers, processors, brewers, distillers, rectifiers, and compounders or [sic] liquors, distilled spirits, and wines or manufacturers of any article of commerce of whatever kind or nature (PDF pp. 45–46 / ord. pp. 41–42)
- **applies_to**: as titled above ("In accordance with the following schedule")
- **basis**: graduated_gross_sales
- **schedule**:

| Amount of Gross Sales/Receipts For the Preceding Calendar Year | Amount of Tax per Annum |
|---|---|
| Less than P10,000.00 | P 247.50 |
| 10,000.00 or more but less than 15,000.00 | 330.00 |
| 15,000.00 or more but less than 20,000.00 | 453.00 |
| 20,000.00 or more but less than 30,000.00 | 660.00 |
| 30,000.00 or more but less than 40,000.00 | 990.00 |
| 40,000.00 or more but less than 50,000.00 | 1,237.50 |
| 50,000.00 or more but less than 75,000.00 | 1,980.00 |
| 75,000.00 or more but less than 100,000.00 | 2,475.00 |
| 100,000.00 or more but less than 150,000.00 | 3,300.00 |
| 150,000.00 or more but less than 200,000.00 | 4,125.00 |
| 200,000.00 or more but less than 300,000.00 | 5,775.00 |
| 300,000.00 or more but less than 500,000.00 | 8,250.00 |
| 500,000.00 or more but less than 750,000.00 | 12,000.00 |
| 750,000.00 or more but less than 1,000,000.00 | 15,000.00 |
| 1,000,000.00 or more but less than 2,000,000.00 | 20,625.00 |
| 2,000,000.00 or more but less than 3,000,000.00 | 24,750.00 |
| 3,000,000.00 or more but less than 4,000,000.00 | 29,700.00 [see layout note] |
| 4,000,000.00 or more but less than 5,000,000.00 | 34,650.00 [see layout note] |
| 5,000,000.00 or more but less than 6,500,000.00 | 36,562.50 [see layout note] |
| 6,500,000.00 or more but less than 50 Million | "The tax at 6.5M plus an additional tax of 37.5% of 1% of the excess of ₱6,500,000.00." |
| 50 Million or more but less than 100 Million | "The tax at 50M plus an additional tax of 30% of 1% of the excess of ₱ 50,000,000.00." |
| 100 Million or more | "The tax at 100M plus an additional tax of 22.5% of 1%." |

- **conditions/notes**:
  - Page-break layout note (legally material): the table spans PDF pp. 45–46. On p. 45 the amount column contains 17 printed amounts against 16 printed brackets, with **29,700.00 printed dangling at the bottom of the column with no bracket text beside it**. On p. 46 the amounts 34,650.00 and 36,562.50 are printed on the baselines of the "3,000,000–4,000,000" and "4,000,000–5,000,000" rows respectively, and the "5,000,000–6,500,000" row has a blank amount cell. The only self-consistent pairing (dangling 29,700.00 → 3M–4M row; amounts shifted down one row on p. 46) is the one shown above, and it also matches the arithmetic progression of the schedule. The literal line-by-line pairing on p. 46 alone would leave 29,700.00 unassigned and the 5M–6.5M bracket with no tax. Flagged for legal confirmation against a certified copy.
  - Text after the table (PDF p. 46): "The preceding rates shall apply only to amount of domestic sales of manufacturers, assemblers, repackers, processors, brewers, distillers, rectifiers and compounders of liquors, distilled spirits, and wines or manufacturers of any article of commerce of whatever kind or nature other than those enumerated under paragraph (c) of this Article."
  - The 15,000–20,000 bracket reads **453.00** (verified at 5× zoom; not 453.75 as an LGC ×1.5 pattern would suggest).
- **unclear**: none illegible; the p. 45/p. 46 column-offset issue above is a layout ambiguity, not an illegibility.

### (c) Exporters, and manufacturers, millers, producers, wholesalers, distributors, dealers or retailers of essential commodities (PDF pp. 47–48 / ord. pp. 43–44)
- **applies_to**: "On exporters, and on manufacturers, millers, producers, wholesalers, distributors. [sic] dealers or retailers of essential commodities enumerated hereunder"
- **basis**: percentage_of_gross (relative rate: one-half of the graduated schedules)
- **schedule**: "at a rate not exceeding one-half (1/2) of the rates prescribed under subsections (a), (b), and (d) of this Article:"

| # | Essential commodity |
|---|---|
| (1) | Rice and Corn; |
| (2) | Wheat or cassava flour, meat, dairy products, locally manufactured, processed or preserved food, sugar, salt and agricultural marine, and fresh water products, whether in their original state or not; |
| (3) | Cooking oil and cooking gas; |
| (4) | Laundry soap, detergents, and medicine; |
| (5) | Agricultural implements, equipment and post-harvest facilities, fertilizers, pesticides, insecticides, herbicides and other farm inputs; |
| (6) | Poultry feeds and other animal feeds; |
| (7) | School supplies; and |
| (8) | Cement |

- **conditions/notes** (PDF p. 48): "For purposes of this provision, the term exporters shall refer to those who are principally engaged in the business of exporting goods and merchandise, as well as manufacturers and producers whose goods or products are both sold domestically and abroad. The amount of export sales shall be excluded from the total sales and shall be subject to the rates not exceeding one half (1/2) of the rates prescribed under paragraphs (a), (b), and (d) of this Article."
- **unclear**: none.

- **applies_to**: banks and other financial institutions

- **basis**: graduated_gross_sales (+ "in excess of" percentage row)
- **schedule**:

|---|---|

| 1,000.00 or more but less than 2,000.00 | 60.00 |
| 2,000.00 or more but less than 3,000.00 | 90.00 |

| 5,000.00 or more but less than 6,000.00 | 180.00 |
| 6,000.00 or more but less than 7,000.00 | 210.00 |
| 7,000.00 or more but less than 8,000.00 | 240.00 |
| 8,000.00 or more but less than 10,000.00 | 300.00 |
| 10,000.00 or more but less than 15,000.00 | 450.00 |
| 15,000.00 or more but less than 20,000.00 | 600.00 |
| 20,000.00 or more but less than 30,000.00 | 900.00 |
| 30,000.00 or more but less than 40,000.00 | 1,200.00 |
| 40,000.00 or more but less than 50,000.00 | 1,500.00 |
| 50,000.00 or more but less than 75,000.00 | 2,250.00 |
| 75,000.00 or more but less than 100,000.00 | 3,000.00 |
| 100,000.00 or more but less than 150,000.00 | 4,500.00 |

| 500,000.00 or more but less than 750,000.00 | 22,500.00 |
| 750,000.00 or more but less than 1,000,000.00 | 30,000.00 |
| 1,000,000.00 or more but less than 2,000,000.00 | 60,000.00 |
| 2 Million or more | "The tax at 2M plus an additional tax of 75% of 1% of the excess of 2,000,000.00." |

- **conditions/notes**: none in section text. (No further Article J sections appear before Article K; the ordinance proceeds directly from this table on PDF p. 54 to Article K on PDF p. 55.)

- **schedule**: none.

- **basis**: percentage_of_gross

# New Revenue Code of the City of Malabon 2016 (Ordinance A10-2016)
# Extract A2 — End of Article K (other business taxes), Article L (Exemptions), Article M (Situs of Tax), Article N (Payment of Business Taxes), Article O (Presumptive Income Level), start of Chapter III

**Source:** PDF pages 56–64 = ordinance pages 52–60 (ordinance page = PDF page − 4).
Transcribed from page images (`png/p-056.png` … `p-064.png`), cross-checked against OCR text.

---
---

## Article L. Exemptions

## Sec. 2L.01 — Exemption (ord. p.52–53 / PDF p.56–57)

- **rule** (verbatim, in full):
  > "Business engaged in the production, manufacture, refining, distribution of oil, gasoline, and other petroleum products shall not be subject to any local tax imposed under Article A and Article B.
  >
  > Delivery trucks, vans or vehicles used by manufacturers, producers, wholesalers, dealers or retailers enumerated under Section 141 of R.A. 7160 shall be exempt from the peddler's tax herein imposed.
  >
  > The tax herein imposed shall be payable within the first twenty (20) days of January. An individual who will start to peddle merchandise or articles of commerce after January 20 shall pay the full amount of the tax before engaging in such activity."
- **notes**:
  - Cross-references: Article A and Article B of Chapter II (business taxes); Section 141 of R.A. 7160 (Local Government Code — peddler's tax).
  - IMPORTANT: on these pages the exemption article lists ONLY (1) petroleum production/manufacture/refining/distribution businesses and (2) delivery trucks/vans of Sec. 141 RA 7160 enterprises (peddler's tax only). No cooperative / BMBE / government exemption appears in this Article on these pages — if such exemptions exist they are elsewhere in the Code.
  - The third paragraph (payment within first 20 days of January; late-starting peddlers pay full amount before engaging) is printed inside Sec. 2L.01 as it appears on the page; it evidently refers to the peddler's tax.

---

## Article M. Situs of Tax

## Sec. 2M.01 — Situs of the Tax (ord. p.53–56 / PDF p.57–60)

- **rule**: "(a) For purposes of collection of the business tax under the 'situs' of the tax law, the following definition of terms and guidelines shall be strictly observed:"

### (a) Definitions

1. **Principal Office** – "the head or main office of the businesses appearing in the pertinent documents submitted to the Securities and Exchange Commission, or the Department of Trade and Industry, or other appropriate agencies as the case may be. The city or municipality specifically mentioned in the articles of the incorporation or official registration papers as being the official address or said principal office shall be considered as the situs thereof. In case there is a transfer or relocation of the principal office to another city or municipality, it shall be the duty of the owner, operator or manager of the business to give due notice of such transfer or relocation to the local chief executives of the cities or municipalities concerned within fifteen (15) days after such transfer or relocation is effected."
2. **Branch or Sales Office** – "a fixed place in a locality which conducts operations of the businesses as an extension of the principal office. However, offices used only as display areas of the products where no stocks or items are stored for sale, although orders for the products may be received thereat, are not branch or sales offices as herein contemplated. A warehouse which accepts orders and/or issues sales invoices independent of a branch with sales office shall be considered as a sales office."
3. **Warehouse** – "a building utilized for the storage of products for sale and from which goods or merchandises are withdrawn for delivery to customers or dealers, or by persons acting on behalf of the business. A warehouse that does not accept orders and/or issue sales invoices as aforementioned shall not be considered a branch or sales office."
4. **Plantation** – "a tract of agricultural land planted to trees or seedlings whether fruit bearing or not, uniformly spaced or seeded by broadcast methods or normally arranged to allow highest production. For purpose of this Article, inland fishing ground shall be considered as plantation."
5. **Experimental Farms** – "agricultural lands utilized by a business or corporation to conduct studies, tests, researches or experiments involving agricultural, agri-business, marine or aquatic livestock, poultry, dairy and other similar products for the purpose of improving the quality and quantity of goods and products. However, on-site sales of commercial quantity made in experimental farms shall be similarly imposed the corresponding tax under paragraph (b), Article 2L.01 of this Ordinance."
   - [note: the printed cross-reference "paragraph (b), Article 2L.01" appears as-is on the page; contextually this likely intends a business-tax provision — recorded verbatim]

### (b) Sales Allocation

1. "All sales made in a locality where there is branch or sales office or warehouse shall be recorded in said branch or sales office or warehouse and the tax shall be payable to the city or municipality where the same is located."
2. "In cases where there is no such branch, sales office, plant or plantation in the locality where the sale is made, the sale shall be recorded in the principal office along with the sale made by said principal office and the tax shall accrue to the city or municipality where said principal office is located."
3. "In cases where there is a factory, project office, plant or plantation in pursuit of business, thirty percent (30%) if all sales recorded in the principal office shall be taxable by the city or municipality where the principal office is located and seventy percent (70%) of all sales recorded in the principal office shall be taxable by the city or municipality where the factory, project office, plant or plantation is located." [Page prints "(30%) if all sales" — evidently a typo for "of all sales"; transcribed verbatim.]
   "The sales allocation in (a) and (b) above shall not apply to experimental farms. LGUs where only experimental farms are located shall not be entitled to the sales allocation herein provided for."
4. "In case of a plantation located in a locality other than that where the factory is located, said seventy percent (70%) sales allocation shall be divided as follows:
   - Sixty percent (60%) to the city or municipality where the factory is located; and
   - Forty percent (40%) to the city or municipality where the plantation is located."
5. "In cases where there are two (2) or more factories, project offices, plants or plantations located in different localities, the seventy percent (70%) sales allocation shall be pro-rated among the localities where such factories, project offices, plants and plantations are located in proportion to their respective volumes of production during the period for which the tax is due. In the case of project offices of services and other independent contractors, the term production shall refer to the costs of projects actually undertaken during the tax period."
6. "The foregoing sales allocation under par. (3) hereof shall be applied irrespective of whether or not sales are made in the locality where the factory, project office, plant or plantation is located. In case of sales made by the factory, project office, plant or plantation, the sale shall be covered by paragraph (1) or (2) above."
7. "In case of manufacturers or producers which engage the services of an independent contractor to produce or manufacture some of their products, the rules on situs of taxation provided in this article as clarified in the paragraphs above shall apply except that the factory or plant and warehouse of the contractor utilized for the production or storage of the manufacturer's products shall be considered as the factory or plant and warehouse of the manufacturer."
8. "All sales made by the factory, project office, plant or plantation located in this city shall be recorded in the branch or sales office which is similarly located herein, and shall be taxable by this city. In case there is no branch or sales office or warehouse in this city, but the principal office is located therein, the sales made in the dais factory shall be taxable by this city along with the sales made in the principal office." [Page prints "dais factory" — evidently a typo for "said factory"; transcribed verbatim.]

### (c) Port of Loading

- "the city or municipality where the port of loading is located shall not levy and collect the tax imposable under Article L, Chapter 2 of this Ordinance unless the exporter maintain in said city or municipality its principal office, a branch, sales office, warehouse, factory, plant or plantation in which case the foregoing rule on the matter shall apply accordingly."

### (d) Route Sales

- "sales made by route trucks, vans or vehicles in this city where a manufacturer, producer, wholesaler, maintains a branch or sales office or warehouse shall be recorded in the branch or sales office or warehouse and shall be taxed herein. This city shall tax the sales of the products withdrawn by route trucks from the branch, sales office or warehouse located herein but sold in another locality."

- **notes**: Cross-references Article 2L.01 (in the experimental-farms definition) and "Article L, Chapter 2" (in Port of Loading). Given the printed Article L is the Exemptions article, these letter references appear internally inconsistent in the ordinance; recorded as printed.

---

## Article N. Payment of Business Taxes

## Sec. 2N.01 — Payment of Business Taxes (ord. p.56 / PDF p.60)

- **rule**:
  - (a) "The taxes imposed under Section 2.J.02 of this Ordinance shall be payable for every separate or distinct establishment or place where the business subject to the tax is conducted and one line of business does not become exempt by being conducted with some other businesses for which such tax has been paid. The tax on a business must be paid by the person conducting the same. The conduct or operation of two or more related businesses provided for under Section 2.J.02 of this Code any one person, natural or juridical, shall require the issuance of a separate permit or license to each business."
  - (b) "In cases where a person conducts or operates two (2) or more of the businesses mentioned in Section 2.J.02 of this Ordinance which are subject to the same rate of imposition, the tax shall be computed on the combined total gross sales or receipts of the said two (2) or more related businesses."
  - (c) "In cases where a person conducts or operates two (2) or more businesses mentioned in Section 2.J.02 of this Ordinance which are subject to different rates of imposition, the taxable gross sales or receipts of each business shall be reported independently and tax thereon shall be computed on the basis of the pertinent schedule."
- **notes**: References Section 2.J.02 (the graduated business tax schedule, Article J of Chapter II).

## Sec. 2N.02 — Accrual of Payment (ord. p.56 / PDF p.60)

- **rule**: "Unless specifically provided in this Article, the taxes imposed herein shall accrue on the first day of January of each year."

## Sec. 2N.03 — Time of Payment (ord. p.56–57 / PDF p.60–61)

- **rule**: "The tax shall be paid once within the first twenty (20) days of January or in quarterly installments within the first twenty (20) days of January, April, July, and October of each year. The Sangguniang Panlungsod may, for a justifiable reason or cause, extend the time for payment of such taxes without surcharges or penalties, but only for a period not exceeding six (6) months."
- **notes**: KEY payment-deadline provision for the fee calculator: annual = on or before Jan 20; quarterly = first 20 days of Jan/Apr/Jul/Oct. Extension power: Sangguniang Panlungsod, max 6 months, without surcharge/penalty.

## Sec. 2N.04 — Administrative Provisions (ord. p.57–59 / PDF p.61–63)

- **rule** (by paragraph):
  - **(a) Requirement.** "Any person who shall establish, operate or conduct any business, trade or activity mentioned in this Chapter in this city shall first obtain a Mayor's Permit and pay the fee therefor and the business tax imposed under the pertinent Article."
  - **(b) Issuance and Posting of Official Receipt.** "The City Treasurer shall issue an official receipt upon payment of the business tax. Issuance of the said official receipt shall not relieve the taxpayer of any requirement imposed by the different departments of this city. Every person issued an official receipt for the conduct of a business or undertaking shall keep the same conspicuously posted in plain view at the place of business or undertaking. If the individual has no fixed place of business or office, he shall keep the official receipt in his person. The receipt shall be produced upon demand by the City Mayor, City Treasurer, or their duly authorized representatives."
  - **(c) Invoices or Receipt.** "All persons subject to the taxes on business shall, for each sale or transfer of merchandise or goods, or for services rendered, valued at Twenty-Five Pesos (P 25.00) or more at any one time, prepare and issue sales or commercial invoices and receipts serially numbered in duplicate, showing among others, their names or styles, if any, and business address. The original of each sales invoice or receipts shall be issued to the purchaser or customer and the duplicate to be kept and preserved by the person subject to the said tax, in his place of business for a period of five (5) years. The receipts or invoices issued pursuant to the requirement of the Bureau of Internal Revenue for determination of national internal revenue taxes shall be sufficient for purposes of this Code."
  - **(d) Sworn Statement of Gross Receipts or Sales.** "Operators of business subject to the taxes on business shall submit a sworn statement of the capital investment before the start of their business operations and upon application for a Mayor's permit to operate the business. Upon payment of the tax levied in this Chapter, any person engaged in business subject to the business tax paid based on gross sales and/or receipts shall submit a sworn statement of his gross sales/receipts for the preceding calendar year or quarter in such manner and form as may be prescribed by the City Treasurer. Should the taxpayer fail to submit a sworn statement of gross sales or receipts, due among others to his failure to have a book of accounts, records or subsidiaries for his business, the City Treasurer or his authorized representatives may verify or assess the gross sales or receipts of the taxpayer under the best available evidence upon which the tax may be based."
  - **(e) Submission of Certified Income Tax Return Copy.** "All persons who are granted a permit to conduct an activity or business and who are liable to pay the business tax provided in this Code shall submit a certified photocopy of their income tax returns (ITR) on or before April 30 of each year. The deficiency in the business tax arising out of the difference in gross receipts or sales declared in the application for Mayor's Permit/Declaration of gross sales or receipts and the gross receipts or sales declared in the ITR shall be payable on or before May 20 of the same year with interest at the rate of ten percent (10%) corresponding to the two percent (2%) per month from January to May. Payments of the deficiency tax made after May 20 shall be subject to the twenty-five percent (25%) surcharge and two percent (2%) interest for every month counted from January up to the month payment is made."
  - **(f) Issuance of Certification.** "The City Treasurer may, upon presentation or satisfactory proof that the original receipt has been lost, stolen or destroyed, issue a certification to the effect that the business tax has been paid, indicating therein, the number of the official receipt issued, upon payment of a fee of One Hundred Fifty Pesos (P150.00)."
  - **(g) Transfer of Business to Other Location.** "Any business for which a city business tax has been paid by the person conducting it may be transferred and continued in any other place within the territorial limits of this city without payment of additional tax during the period for which the payment of the tax was made."
  - **(h) Retirement of Business.**
    1. "Any person natural or juridical, subject to the tax on business under Article L, Chapter 2 of this Ordinance shall, upon termination of the business, submit a sworn statement of the gross sales or receipts for the current calendar year within thirty (30) days following the closure. Any tax due shall first be paid before any business or undertaking is fully terminated. For the purposes hereof, termination shall mean that business operations are stopped completely. Any change in ownership, management and/or name of the business shall not constitute termination as herein contemplated. Unless stated otherwise, assumption of the business by any new owner or manager or re-registration of the same business under a new name will only be considered by the LGU concerned for record purposes in the course of the renewal of the permit or license to operate the business.
       The City Treasurer shall see to it that the payment of taxes of a business is not avoided by simulating the termination or retirement thereof. For this purpose, the following procedural guidelines shall be strictly followed:
       a. The City Treasurer shall assign every application for the termination or retirement of business to an inspector in his office who shall go to address of the business on record to verify if it is really not operating. If the inspector finds that the business is simply placed under a new name, manager and/or new owner, the City Treasurer shall recommend to the City Mayor the disapproval of the application of the termination or retirement of said business;
       b. Accordingly, the business continues to become liable for the payment of all taxes, fees, and charges imposed thereon under existing local tax ordinance; and
       c. In addition, in case of a new owner to whom the business was transferred by sale or other form of conveyance, said new owner shall be liable to pay the tax or fee for the business and shall secure a new Mayor's permit therefor."
    2. "In case it is found that the retirement or termination of the business is legitimate and the tax paid during the current year be less than the tax due for the current year based on the gross sales or receipts, the difference in the amount of the tax shall be paid before the business is considered officially retired or terminated."
    3. "The permit issued to a business retiring or terminating its operation shall be surrendered to the City Treasurer who shall forthwith cancel the same and record such cancellation in his books."
  - **(i) Death of Licensee.** "When any individual paying a business tax dies, and the business is continued by a person interested in his estate, no additional payment shall be required for the residue of the term for which the tax was paid."
- **notes**:
  - (e) is the deficiency-tax penalty rule: 10% interest (i.e., 2%/month Jan–May) if paid by May 20; after May 20 → 25% surcharge + 2% interest per month counted from January to month of payment.
  - (h)1 again cites "Article L, Chapter 2" for the business tax (see Article M note about internally inconsistent letter references).
  - No standalone general surcharge/interest section for ordinary late payment appears on these pages; the general 25% surcharge / 2%-per-month interest rule for delinquent taxes is presumably in the Code's general penal/collection provisions elsewhere.

---

## Article O. Presumptive Income Level

## Sec. 2O.01 — Presumptive Income Level (ord. p.60 / PDF p.64)

- **rule**: "For every tax period, the Treasurer's Office shall prepare a stratified schedule of 'presumptive income level' to approximate the gross receipt of each business classification."

## Sec. 2O.24 — Administrative Provisions (ord. p.60 / PDF p.64)

- **rule**: "(a) In case taxpayer cannot provide or present books of accounts for its operations or if the Office of the City Treasurer has no other means of ascertaining the correct actual gross sales of the taxpayer, the City Treasurer shall determine the taxable gross receipts by applying the Presumptive Income Level (PIL) provided in this Code, and thereafter assess and collect the tax due based on the Schedule provided herein:
  **SCHEDULE OF MINIMUM GROSS SALES**¹"
- **schedule**: NONE PRINTED. The heading "SCHEDULE OF MINIMUM GROSS SALES" carries footnote 1 but NO table rows appear on this page (Sec. 2O.03 follows immediately). Footnote 1 (verbatim): "The Schedule may vary upon proper evaluation of the City Treasury and approval of the Local Finance Committee." The PIL schedule is therefore delegated to the City Treasury / Local Finance Committee rather than fixed in the ordinance text at this location.
- **notes**: The section is numbered "2O.24" on the page even though it sits between 2O.01 and 2O.03 — almost certainly a typo for 2O.02; recorded as printed.

## Sec. 2O.03 — [untitled] (ord. p.60 / PDF p.64)

- **rule**: "The Presumptive Income Level (PIL) of gross receipts shall be used to validate the gross receipts declared by taxpayers and/or for establishing the taxable gross receipts where no valid data is otherwise available."

# Chapter III — Permit and Regulatory Fees, Article A: Mayor's Permit Fee on Business

## Sec. 3A.01 — Mayor's Permit
- **applies_to**: All persons conducting business within the City.
- **basis**: n/a (declaratory)
- **schedule**: none.
- **conditions/notes**: "All persons are required to obtain a Mayor's Permit for the privilege of conducting business within the City." (PDF p.64 / ord. p.60)
- **unclear**: none.

## Sec. 3A.02 — Administrative Provisions
- **applies_to**: Any person (natural or juridical) establishing, operating or conducting any business mentioned in this Article.
- **basis**: fixed (application filing fee)
- **schedule**:

| Item | Fee |
|---|---|
| Application filing fee, payable to City Mayor through the Business Permits and Licensing Office (BPLO) before processing of the business application | Php 100.00 |

- **conditions/notes**:
  - a) Any person who shall establish, operate or conduct any business mentioned in this Article shall first secure a Mayor's Permit and pay the corresponding fee therefor.
  - b) Written application submitted to the City Mayor through the BPLO; filing fee of One Hundred Pesos (Php100.00) required before processing. (PDF pp.64–65 / ord. pp.60–61)
- **unclear**: none.

## Sec. 3A.03 — Imposition of Fee
- **applies_to**: Issuance of a Mayor's Permit to operate a business, pursue an occupation or calling, or undertake an activity within the city.
- **basis**: mixed — **fixed** annual fee per business type; **per_unit** for some (per pump, per stall, per seat, per machine, per branch); **bracket** for area-based (warehouse, parking, offices, lessors), storeys/doors (lessors), stalls (markets), and capitalization (branch/sales-outlet fees).
- **conditions/notes** (intro, PDF p.65 / ord. p.61):
  - There shall be collected an **annual fee** for the issuance of a Mayor's Permit.
  - "The permit fee is payable for every distinct or separate business or place where the business or trade is conducted. One line of business of [sic] trade does not become exempt by being conducted with some other business of [sic] trade for which the permit fee has been obtained and the corresponding fee paid for." (multiple lines of business each pay.)

## Sec. 3A.04 — Time and Manner of Payment
- **applies_to**: All Mayor's Permit fees under this Article.
- **basis**: n/a (payment rules)
- **schedule**: none.
- **conditions/notes** (PDF p.73 / ord. p.69):
  - Fee paid to the **City Treasurer upon application**, before any business or undertaking can be lawfully begun or pursued.
  - **Renewal**: within the **first twenty (20) days of January** of each year.
  - **New business starting after January 20**: "the fee shall be reckoned from the beginning of the calendar quarter" (prorated by quarter of start).
  - **Abandonment/retirement**: "When the business or activity is abandoned, the fee shall not be exacted for a period longer than the end of the calendar quarter. If the fee has been paid for a period longer than the current quarter and the business activity is abandoned, **no refund** of the fee corresponding to the unexpired quarter or quarters shall be made."
- **unclear**: none.

## Sec. 3A.05 — Administrative Provisions
- **applies_to**: Applicants for and holders of Mayor's Permits.
- **basis**: n/a (procedural; contains one fixed fee — duplicate permit).
- **schedule**:

| Item | Fee |
|---|---|
| Issuance of duplicate permit (original lost, stolen or destroyed), upon satisfactory proof | "One Hundred Pesos (P 150.00)" [?] — words say One Hundred, figures say 150.00 (PDF p.76) |

- **conditions/notes** (PDF pp.73–77 / ord. pp.69–73):
  - **(a) Supervision and control over establishments and places.** City Mayor shall supervise and regulate all establishments and places where business is conducted; may prescribe rules and regulations to maintain peaceful, healthy, and sanitary conditions in the municipality.
  - **(b) Application for Mayor's Permit; False Statement.** Application filed with the Office of the City Mayor on its prescribed form; must set forth requisite information (name and residence of applicant, description of business or undertaking, other data required).
    - **1. For a newly-started business** (documentary requirements, PDF pp.73–74):
      - a. Location sketch of the new business
      - b. [?] not visible in scan (list jumps from a. at foot of PDF p.73 to c. at top of PDF p.74)
      - c. Department of Trade and Industry (DTI) Registration Certificate with Approved Application Forms, in case of single proprietorship
      - d. Securities and Exchange Commission (SEC) Registration and Articles of Incorporation and By-Laws, in case of partnership or corporation
      - e. A certificate attesting to the tax exemption if the business is exempt
      - f. Certification from the officer in charge of the zoning that the location of the new business is in accordance with zoning regulations
      - g. Tax clearance showing that the applicant has paid his tax obligations to the municipality
      - h. Barangay clearance/proof of filing (in case of non-issuance of barangay clearance within seven (7) working days from date of filing a Mayor's Permit may be issued to the applicant)
      - i. Three (3) passport size pictures of the owner or operator or in cases of a partnership or corporation the picture of the senior or managing partners and that of the President or General Manager
      - j. Health certificate for all food handlers, and those required under Chapter IV, Art. D of this Revenue Code
      - k. Community Tax Certificate
      - l. Contract of Lease, if leasing
    - **2. For renewal of existing business permits** (PDF pp.74–75; lettering as printed is inconsistent — a, b, b, c, then d–g):
      - a. Previous year's Mayor's permit
      - b. Copies of the annual or quarterly tax payments
      - b) [sic] Copies of all receipts showing payment of all regulatory fees as provided for in this Code
      - c) Certificate of tax exemption from local taxes or fees, if exempt
      - d) Audited Financial Statement prescribed by the Bureau of Internal Revenue for the next preceding year
      - e) BIR Registration Certificate
      - f) Barangay Clearance
      - g) Declaration of previous year's gross sales/receipts
    - Upon submission, authorities verify compliance with other city requirements (sanitary, power and light installation, other safety requirements). **Permit issued only upon compliance** with safety requirements and **after payment of corresponding inspection fees** and other impositions of the Revenue Code and other city tax ordinances.
    - Any **false statement** deliberately made by the applicant is sufficient ground for **denying or revoking** the permit; applicant/licensee may be prosecuted per this Article's penalties.
    - **A Mayor's Permit shall NOT be issued to** (PDF p.75):
      1. Any person who previously violated an ordinance or regulation governing permits granted;
      2. Any person whose business establishment or undertaking does not conform with zoning regulations, and safety, health and other requirements of the municipality;
      3. Any person who has unsettled tax obligation, debt or other liability to the government;
      4. Any person who is disqualified under any provision of law or ordinance to establish or operate the business applied for.
    - Likewise, a permit shall be **denied** to any applicant who declares gross sales or receipts **manifestly below industry standards or the Presumptive Income Level** established in the municipality for the same or closely similar activity/business.
  - **(c) Issuance of Permit; Contents of Permit** (PDF pp.75–76). Upon approval, two (2) copies of the application duly signed by the City Mayor are returned to the applicant; one (1) copy presented to the City Treasurer as basis for collection of the Mayor's Permit fee and corresponding business tax. Permit issued by the City Mayor upon presentation of the receipt for payment of the Mayor's Permit and the official receipt issued by the City Treasurer for payment of the business tax. Every permit shows: name and residence of applicant; nationality and marital status; nature of organization (sole proprietorship, corporation or partnership, etc.); location of business; date of issue and expiration; other necessary information. Duplicate permit on loss/theft/destruction upon payment (see schedule above).
  - **(d) Posting of Permit** (PDF p.76). Permit kept conspicuously posted at all times in the place of business/office; if none, kept on the person; produced immediately upon demand by the City Mayor, City Treasurer or their duly authorized representatives.
  - **(e) Duration of Permit and Renewal** (PDF p.76). Granted for not more than one (1) year; **expires on the thirty-first (31st) of December** following date of issuance unless revoked or surrendered earlier; ceases upon revocation or surrender; **renewed within the first twenty (20) days of January**; continuing validity only upon renewal and payment of the corresponding fee.
  - **(f) Revocation of Permit** (PDF pp.76–77). The Mayor's Permit may be revoked on any of the following grounds:
    1. When a person doing business under the provisions of this Revenue Code violates any of its provisions;
    2. When the person refuses to pay an indebtedness or liability to the municipality;
    3. [?] not visible in scan (text jumps from ground 2 at foot of PDF p.76 to ground 4 at top of PDF p.77);
    4. When the person abuses his privilege to do business to the injury of the public moral or peace; or
    5. When a place where such business is established is being conducted in a disorderly or unlawful manner, is a nuisance, or is permitted to be used as a resort for disorderly characters, criminals or women of ill-repute.
    6. Such revocation shall operate to **forfeit all sums** which may have been paid in respect of said privilege, in addition to fines and imprisonment that may be imposed by the Court, and to prohibit the exercise of the [privilege] by the person whose privilege is revoked, until restored by the Sangguniang Panlungsod. (Text as printed: "...to prohibit the exercise of the by the person whose privilege is revoked, until restore by the Sangguniang Panlungsod.")
- **unclear**:
  - [?] PDF p.74: newly-started-business requirement item "b." not visible (page-break gap).
  - [?] PDF pp.74–75: renewal requirements lettering duplicated/inconsistent (a, b, b, c, d, e, f, g as printed).
  - [?] PDF p.76: duplicate-permit fee — "One Hundred Pesos (P 150.00)" (words vs. figures conflict).

## Sec. 3A.06 — Rules and Regulations on Certain Establishments
- **applies_to**: Food establishments; establishments selling cooked/readily edible foods; sauna bath, massage, barber and beauty shops.
- **basis**: n/a (regulatory conditions on permit holders)
- **schedule**: none.
- **conditions/notes** (PDF p.77 / ord. p.73):
  - (a) Restaurants, cafes, cafeterias, carinderias, eateries, food caterers, ice cream and other refreshment parlors, soda fountain bars: no owner shall employ any cook or food dispenser without a **Food Handler's Certificate** from the City Health Officer, **renewable every six (6) months**.
  - (b) Establishments selling cooked and readily edible foods: food adequately covered and protected from dust, flies and other insects; strict compliance with sanitation rules promulgated by the City Health Officer and existing laws or ordinances.
  - (c) Sauna bath, massage, barber and beauty shops: shall not operate with masseurs, barbers, and beauticians without the necessary corresponding **medical certificate** from the City Health Officer.

## Article C — Building Permit (PDF 83–84, ord. 79–80)

### Sec. 3C.01 — Imposition of Fee
- **applies_to**: Each applicant for a building permit.
- **basis**: Fees pursuant to Department Order 155, Series of 1992, dated September 25, 1992, of the Department of Public Works and Highways, and the National Building Code.
- **schedule**: No fee table printed in this Article — fees adopt DPWH D.O. 155 s. 1992 / National Building Code schedules by reference. (A detailed schedule appears later under Article V, Sec. 3V.01, ord. p.125 ff.)
- **conditions/notes**: None beyond the reference.

### Sec. 3C.02 — Time and Payment
- The fees specified under this article shall be paid to the City Treasurer upon application for a building permit from the City Mayor.

### Sec. 3C.03 — Administrative Provisions
- To obtain a building permit, applicant files a written application on the prescribed form with the Office of the Mayor/Building Official, providing:
  1. A description of the work to be covered by the permit applied for;
  2. Description and ownership of the lot on which the proposed work is to be done as evidenced by TCT and/or copy of the contract of lease over the lot if the applicant is not the registered owner;
  3. The use or occupancy for which the proposed work is intended;
  4. Estimated cost of the proposed work.
- To be submitted together with such application: at least five sets of corresponding plans and specifications prepared, signed and sealed by a duly licensed architect or civil engineer (architectural and structural plans), a registered mechanical engineer (mechanical plans), a registered electrical engineer (electrical plans), and by licensed sanitary engineer or master plumber (plumbing or sanitary installation plans), except in those cases exempted or not required by the Building Official.

### Sec. 3C.04 — Penal Provisions (PDF 84, ord. 80)
- Unlawful for any person, firm or corporation to erect, construct, enlarge, alter, repair, move, improve, remove, convert, demolish, equip, use, occupy, or maintain any building or structure, or cause the same to be done, contrary to or in violation of any provision of the Building Code.
- Penalty upon conviction: fine not more than twenty thousand pesos, or imprisonment of not more than two years, or both. For a corporation, firm, partnership or association, the penalty shall be imposed ["shall bed imposed" [sic]] upon its official responsible for such violation; if the guilty party is an alien, he shall immediately be deported after payment of the fine and/or service of sentence.

---
## Article D — Permit Fee for Zoning/Locational Clearance (PDF 84–86, ord. 80–82)

### Sec. 3.D.01 — Imposition of Fees
- **applies_to**: Owners and/or contractors of land development, construction, renovation, and expansion projects (zoning and/or locational clearance for land use); item (d) applies to businesses.
- **basis**: Per application (filing), per verification (by use category), per sq.m. of total floor area (processing), or flat per-business fees.

**schedule**:

a) Application/Filing Fee — for every application for locational clearance irrespective of whether approved or not, motions for reconsideration, reclassification and for filing of complaint:

| Item | Fee (P) |
|---|---|
| 1) Locational clearance | 45.00 |
| 2) Motion for reconsideration | 330.00 |
| 3) Petition/request for reclassification | 660.00 |
| 4) For filing complaint, except those involving pauper-litigant which shall be free of charge | 100.00 |

- Note (printed between items 3 and 4): This excludes the cost of reclassification proceeding such as Production/reproduction of maps and other documents, public hearings and publication, which likewise shall be charged to the applicant/proponent.

b) Zoning and Land Use Verification Fee (PDF 85, ord. 81):

| Item | Fee (P) |
|---|---|
| 1) Residential | 110.00 |
| 2) Commercial and industrial | 300.00 |
| 3) Social, Educational, Institutional | 110.00 |
| 4) Ancillary | according to the category of principal building/structure |

c) Processing Fee (per sq.m. of total floor area):

| Item | Fee (P per sq.m.) |
|---|---|
| 1) Residential (single detached and duplex type) | 9.00 |
| 2) Commercial establishments including apartments, mass housing, town houses constructed primarily for grain [sic — likely "gain"] purposes | 18.00 |
| 3) Industrial Establishments | 18.00 |
| 4) Institutional (schools, hospitals, etc.) | 9.00 |
| 5) Memorial parks/cemeteries | 9.00 |
| 6) Agro-industrial: (a) Manufacturing | 18.00 |
| 6) Agro-industrial: (b) Non-manufacturing | 18.00 |
| 7) Telecommunication/Cell sites/Communication Facilities: (a) Less than 2 Million Pesos of the total project | 5,000.00 |
| 7) (b) Over 2 Million Pesos on the total project cost | 5,000.00 + 1% of the total Amount in excess of 2 Million |
| 8) Billboards | 9.00 |
| 9) Yards utilized for commercial purposes | 2.70 |
| 10) Yards utilized for industrial purposes | 4.50 |
| 11) All types of renovation | 75% of prescribed rates |

- 12) Payment under schedule (a) shall be made prior to acceptance of application.
- 13) Payment under schedule (b) shall be made prior to the release of approved application.
- 14) Government projects except government owned or controlled corporations are exempted from paying the prescribed fees.

d) On business — Locational clearance/certificate of conformance and certificate of non-conformance (PDF 85 heading, table on PDF 86, ord. 82):

| Item | Fee (P) |
|---|---|
| 1) Land use verification fee | 345.00 |
| 2) Processing Fee | 345.00 |
| 3) Sari-sari stores | 345.00 |
| 4) All other businesses | 345.00 |

### Sec. 3.D.02 — Application and Certification (PDF 86, ord. 82)
- A.) Buildings — Applicants for locational clearance for conforming project shall submit their application to the Office of the Local Zoning Administrator. (Approved Citizen's Charter of Malabon for the process flow)
- B.) Business — Applicants for locational clearance for conforming activity shall submit their application to the "Officer in charge" at one stop shop for verification of submitted documents and payment of filing fee. (Approved Citizen's Charter of Malabon for the process flow)
- C.) The locational clearance issued for the construction/renovation activity cannot be used for the proposed business activity to be commenced within the constructed/renovated structure.
- D.) A certificate of non-conformance of all non-conforming users shall secure [sic] from the Office of the Local Zoning Administrator within one (1) year from the approval of the amended Malabon Zoning Ordinance. This certificate shall be valid for a period of One (1) year from the date of its issuance, after which shall be renewed yearly for a period specific under the amended Zoning Ordinance.

### Sec. 3.D.03 — Penalty & Surcharge (PDF 86, ord. 82)
1. Surcharge of twenty five percent (25%) based [on] and added to the corresponding processing fee, plus interest of fourteen percent (14%) per annum, imposed on all structures/operations without any locational clearance, Certificate of conformance/non-conformance, building permit, license or permit to operate, upon implementation of the amended zoning ordinance.
2. Penal Provisions — any person found in violation of this ordinance shall be liable to pay a fine of not less than One Thousand pesos (Php 1,000.00) for the first offense; Three Thousand Pesos (Php 3,000.00) for the second offense; and Five Thousand pesos (Php 5,000.00) for the third and succeeding offense. Person who shall fail to pay a fine may be prosecuted before the courts and upon conviction be sentenced to a penalty of sixty days (60) imprisonment and a fine of five thousand pesos (Php 5,000.00).
   - If the violation is committed by a firm, corporation or partnership or judicial person, the manager, managing partner, director or any other officer charged with the management of such firm, corporation or judicial person, if found to have committed any violation of this ordinance, shall be criminally responsible as provided for in this section.

- Sec. 3L.03 Time of Payment: impounding fee paid to the City Treasurer prior to release of the impounded animal to its owner. Sec. 3L.04 Administrative Provisions: Barangay Tanods authorized to apprehend/impound; 7-day posting; unclaimed animals auctioned (owner may stop sale by paying fees + costs); proceeds satisfy costs, residue to General Fund; item 4 (top of PDF 109): if not disposed of within seven (7) days from the date of notice of public auction, the animal shall be considered sold to the City Government for the amount equivalent to the poundage fees due.

# Chapter III, Article V — Building Permit, Certificate of Occupancy and Other Similar Fees

**Source:** New Revenue Code of the City of Malabon 2016 (City Ordinance A10-2016).

The total area is split up into sub-areas corresponding to the area bracket indicated in the Table above. Each sub-area and the fee corresponding to its area bracket are multiplied together. The total building fee is the sum of the individual products…"
- Sample computation (3,200 sq. m building; ord. p. 126–127 / PDF 130–131):
  - First 500 sq. m @ 23.00 = 11,500.00; next 100 @ 22.00 = 2,200.00; next 100 @ 20.50 = 2,050.00; next 100 @ 19.50 = 1,950.00; next 100 @ 18.00 = 1,800.00; next 100 @ 17.00 = 1,700.00; next 500 @ 16.00 = 8,000.00; next 500 @ 15.00 = 7,500.00; next 1,000 @ 14.00 = 14,000.00; last 200 @ 12.00 = 2,400.00 — **Total Building Fee P 53,100.00**.
  - [?] The @-rate lines are garbled by line-wrap across the page break (PDF 130→131); the peso amounts column (verified from image) is consistent with the sub-area split above.

2. Table II.G.1 (PDF 129): first group-column header ends with a trailing comma ("A, B, C, D, E, G, H,") — final letter (presumably I) cut off.
3. Division A-2 (PDF 130): "Above 100 to 150 sq. m" bracket absent from print (rows jump 50–100 → above 150).
4. Item 10.d.v — Certificate of Occupancy, Division J-1, above 5,000 up to 10,000 sq. m (PDF 143): prints **200.00**; DPWH DO 155 standard is 600.00. Possible misprint.
5. Item 11.b.iv — Annual inspection, above 200 up to 350 sq. m (PDF 144): prints **80.00**, breaking progression; DO 155 has 480.00. Possible dropped digit.
6. Item 8.m (PDF 139): excess-over-10-cu.m rate prints 480.00 (same as base; DO 155 has 24.00); heading prints "except for Commercial/Industrial Use" vs DO 155 "for Commercial/Industrial Use".
7. Cost-bracket upper bounds in items 10.a.iii / 10.b.i / 10.b.iii / 10.c.iii (PDF 143): cut off at line-wrap ("up to" with no amount); reconstructed as P150,000/P850,000 from the parallel schedules.
8. Plumbing fixture names, 6.b and 6.c (PDF 135–136), and elevator names 5.c.i–ii (PDF 134), and cinematograph lines 11.c (PDF 145): trailing nouns truncated at the right margin of the printed page; bracketed completions per DO 155.
9. 6.d "Each water meter" header line carries an anomalous "P 2.00" (PDF 136).
10. 9.b(c)/(d) (PDF 140): descriptive text cut at line/page break after "issuance of" and "foundation with".
11. 11.e.ii (PDF 145): cross-referenced section number not printed ("same as in Section ___").
12. 11.f.iii(c) (PDF 145): prints "above 500 tons" where series suggests "above 150 tons".

# PART 7 — Chapter III, Articles S, T, U: Film-Making, Liquor, Tobacco/Cigarettes *(source extract A4, continued)*

> **Assembly note:** Placed after Article V per assembly plan; in the ordinance these articles (ord. pp. 123–125) precede Article V (ord. p. 125 ff.). The '[?]' notes block at the end covers all pages of extract A4 (Parts 5 and 7).

## Article S — Permit Fee on Film-Making (PDF 127, ord. 123) — incidental, full text on assigned page

### Sec. 3S.01 — Imposition of Fee
- **applies_to**: Any person who shall go on location-filming within the territorial jurisdiction of this city.
- **basis**: Per filming, per day, by kind of activity.

**schedule**:

| KINDS OF ACTIVITY | Rate of Fee per Filming (Per Day) (P) |
|---|---|
| a. Commercial movies | 5,000.00 |
| b. Commercial advertisements | 5,000.00 |
| c. Documentary film | 2,500.00 |
| d. Videotape coverage | 2,500.00 |

- In cases of extension of filming time, the additional amount required must be paid prior to extension to filming time.

### Sec. 3S.02 — Exemption
- Exemption may be granted to applicant for Mayor's Permit upon formal request to the Office of the City Mayor.

### Sec. 3S.03 — Time of Payment
- The fee imposed herein shall be paid to the City Treasurer upon application for the Mayor's Permit five (5) days before location-filming is commenced.

---

## Article T — Special Permit to Sell/Serve Liquor and Alcoholic Drinks (PDF 127–128, ord. 123–124)

### Sec. 3T.01 — Liquor Permit
- **applies_to**: Establishments and/or individuals engaged in the manufacture, retail and/or wholesale of liquor or intoxicating beverages.
- Shall secure a permit from the Business Permit and Licensing Office (BPLO), Office of the Mayor, before producing/serving/dispensing/selling the same.

### Sec. 3T.02 — Application/Filing Fee
- **applies_to**: Any person desiring to produce/sell/serve and/or dispense liquor; application filed in prescribed form with the BPLO upon payment of filing fee.
- **basis**: Flat filing fee by activity class.

**schedule**:

| Class | Filing Fee (Php) |
|---|---|
| Retailer | 150.00 |
| Wholesaler | 300.00 |
| Serving/dispensing | 750.00 |
| Manufacture/Distiller/Producer | 1,000.00 |
| Special Permit (for amusement places) | 5,000.00 |

### Sec. 3T.03 — Special Permit Fee for Selling/Serving Liquor during Prohibited Time (PDF 128, ord. 124)
- No licensed liquor dealer shall sell or serve liquor before 8:00 a.m. to [sic] 10:00 p.m., except for sari-sari store allowed only from 8:00 a.m. to 6:00 p.m.
- Special Permit may be issued upon payment of an annual fee of Two Thousand Pesos (Php2,000.00) for a qualified establishment other than amusement places, to be allowed to sell liquor from 10:00 p.m. but not beyond 2:00 a.m.
- Special permit may be issued to qualified amusement places to serve/dispense liquor beyond 10:00 p.m. but not beyond 3:00 a.m., upon payment of Four Thousand Pesos (Php4,000.00) per annum.
- "If [sic — 'It'] shall be unlawful for any person to sell, buy or otherwise dispense of any kind of hard drinks or liquor from any sari-sari store within the jurisdiction of Malabon City from 6:00 p.m. to 8:00 a.m."

**Regulatory Fee (In Addition to the Special Permit Fee):**

| Kinds of Liquor | Regulatory Fee per Annum — Wholesale (P) | Regulatory Fee per Annum — Retail (P) |
|---|---|---|
| Imported Liquor | 4,000.00 | 2,000.00 |

Regulatory Fee in Amusement Places like night club, bar, cocktail lounge, beer garden, disco house, videoke bar, cabaret, ballroom dancing halls, sing-a-long pub houses and other similar establishments:

| Kinds of Liquor | Fee (P) |
|---|---|
| Imported Liquor | 5,000.00 |

### Sec. 3T.04 — Prohibited Areas
No liquor [permit — text reads "No liquor shall be issued"] shall be issued to any person whose place of business is:
- a) Within the radius of fifty (50) meters from an academic school, church, hospital or public building, in the case of bar, cocktail lounge, pubhouse, beer garden, and the like.
- b) Within a radius of two hundred (200) meters from an academic school, church, hospital or public building, in the case of night club, cabaret, and the like.
- c) Within residential areas of the city, in the case of manufacturing, distilling, producing, and the like.

### Sec. 3T.05 — Time of Payment (bottom of PDF 128, ord. 124)
- The fee herein imposed shall be paid to the City Treasurer before the release of the special permit and renewable annually on or before the twentieth (20th) day of January of each year.

---

## Article U — Special Permit to Sell Tobacco and/or Cigarettes (PDF 129, ord. 125)

### Sec. 3U.01 — Payment of Fees
- **applies_to**: Any person, before engaging in the sale and manufacture of tobacco or cigarettes; must first secure a Mayor's Permit from the Business and Licensing Office (BPLO), Office of the Mayor, and pay the corresponding permit fees to the City Treasurer.
- **basis**: Annual flat fee by nature of business.

**schedule**:

| Nature of Business | Rate of Fee/Annum (P) |
|---|---|
| Wholesale | 200.00 |
| Retail | 50.00 |

- **conditions/notes**: Manufacturers shall be taxed under Section 2K.02, and the Permit Fee should be based under Section 3A.03, of this Ordinance.

### Sec. 3U.02 — Time of Payment
- The above imposition shall be paid annually and renewable on or before twentieth (20th) day of January of each year.
