# ARTICLE W. ENVIRONMENTAL PROTECTION AND PRESERVATION FEES

## Sec. 3W.01 — Environmental Protection and Preservation Fees
*(ordinance p. 143 / PDF p. 147)*

- **applies_to**: "Business, industrial, commercial or agricultural establishments with a minimum capitalization of One Hundred Thousand Pesos (Php. 100,000.00) and other establishments."
- **basis**: Annual environmental protection and preservation fee (charged with business permit; see 3W.02 and 3W.06).
- **schedule**: none in this section (imposition clause only; schedule is in 3W.02).
- **conditions/notes**: "Said fee will supplement other sources of revenues for the operations, equipment and maintenance of the City Environmental and Natural Resources Office (CENRO) and the city's environmental protection projects and activities."
- **unclear**: none.

## Sec. 3W.02 — Imposition of Fees
*(ordinance pp. 144-146 / PDF pp. 148-150)*

- **applies_to**: Establishments requiring a Certificate of Environment Clearance (CEC) or Certificate of Non-Coverage (NCC) from Malabon CENRO; two brackets by business type: (I) high-risk industries, (II) all industries/establishments/institutions/offices/agencies that generate waste or pollution of any kind.
- **basis**: Flat annual fee bracketed by business type/activity (not by size or capitalization, except the Php100,000 capitalization threshold in 3W.01).
- **schedule**:

**Headline fee:**

| Item | Fee |
|---|---|
| Certificate of Environment Clearance (CEC) (Same schedule as Inspection Fees) | Php. 2,500.00 |

**Schedule of Fees:**

**I. All High-Risk Industries and Establishments**

| # | Establishment type | Fee (Php) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Fuel Depot & Fuel Storage Facilities | 2,500.00 |
| 2 | Gasoline Service, LPG and auto LPG filling/refilling stations | 2,500.00 |
| 3 | All Big Scale Manufacturing Industries | 2,500.00 |
| 4 | Garbage Contractors, Terminal of Garbage Trucks, Garbage Transfer Station | 2,500.00 |
| 5 | Private Hospitals and Medical and Dental Clinics and Laboratories | 2,500.00 |
| 6 | Shopping Centers, Malls and Markets | 2,500.00 |
| 7 | Junkshops | 2,500.00 |
| 8 | Retailer of LPG | 2,500.00 |

**II. All Industries, Establishments, Institutions, Offices, Agencies that generate waste/pollution of any kind/form (Water, Air, Odor, Noise, Smoke and Land Pollutions, so forth)**

| # | Establishment type | Fee (Php) |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Small-Scale Manufacturing Industries | 1,000.00 |
| 2 | Housing Development Projects such as Residential Subdivisions, Parks (Memorial Park included) Condominiums, Townhouses | 2,500.00 |
| 3 | Funeral Parlors and Cremation Services | 2,500.00 |
| 4 | High-Rise Building | 2,500.00 |
| 5 | Medium Rise Building | 1,000.00 |
| 6 | Fastfood Chains or Quick Service Restaurants | 2,500.00 |
| 7 | Catering Services, commissaries | 2,500.00 |
| 8 | Vehicle Terminal/Garage of Transport/Trucking Services | 2,500.00 |
| 9 | Hotels, Motels, Apartels, Inns and similar establishments | 2,500.00 |
| 10 | Water refilling Stations | 1,500.00 |
| 11 | Amusement places such as KTV, Videoke bars, beer houses and the like | 2,500.00 |
| 12 | Welding shops, machine shops, vehicle repair and repainting shops; Refrigerator and air conditioning repair shops | 2,500.00 |
| 12 (cont., unnumbered line) | Furniture shops and sash factories, fabricators (steel, concrete) | 2,500.00 |
| 13 | Warehouses, storage facilities | 2,500.00 |
| 14 | Car Wash Shops | 1,000.00 |
| 15 | Animal Farm like piggery, Animal Breeding Services | 2,500.00 |
| 16 | Schools, Montessori's, learning centers, review centers and other similar establishments | 2,500.00 |
| 17 | Laundry shops and dry cleaning services | 2,500.00 |
| 18 | Haberdashery, tailoring, clothes manufacturing services and others | 1,500.00 |
| 19 | Three-door apartments and up | 1,000.00 |
| 20 | Such other activities and projects as may be determined by CENRO officers | 500.00 |

- **conditions/notes**:
  - "A Certificate of Environment Clearance (CEC) or Certificate of Non-Coverage (NCC) will only be issued after compliance with the environmental requirements of the Malabon CENRO and the payment of the required fees indicated herein."
  - "Additional fees may be charged against or required from any business operator or owner if deemed necessary by the Malabon CENRO after an inspection in accordance with the provision set by existing national environmental laws and city ordinances."
  - "Penalties must be settled with the City Environmental and Natural Resources Office (CENRO) and payment at the Office of the Treasurer within five working days upon receipt of the Ordinance Violation Receipt (OVR)."
  - Same-nature rule: "In the case of a single person/proprietorship, a partnership or corporation conducts or operates two or more businesses of the same nature or type in one barangay, the environmental protection fee with the higher/highest rate shall be imposed on the business."
  - Different-nature rule: "In the case of a single person/proprietorship, partnership or corporation or operating two or more business of different nature or type, or as may be determined by the inspection officer, in one office with the same address, separate environmental protection fees shall be imposed on each business undertaking."
  - PCO rule: "All of the abovementioned institutions, organizations and business establishments shall appoint and identify their respective Pollution Control Officer (PCO) the name of whom must be submitted to the CENR within 30 days after the inspection. The said PCO must attend a DENR-conducted and accredited PCO training for the proper accreditation." (Text reads "CENR", sic.)
  - DENR-EMB rule: "All business establishments dealing in high-risk industries and business activities which are required by the Department of Environment and Natural Resources (DENR)-Environmental Management Bureau (EMB) to secure a Permit To Operate, Environmental Clearance Certificate, Permit to Discharge Wastewater, Permit to Operate Generators, and other similar permits, clearances, certificates and licenses issued by the National Government Offices shall submit a copy of the abovementioned documents to the Malabon CENRO along with their business permit applications before any CENRO clearance will be issued." (spans onto ordinance p. 146)
- **unclear**:
  - [?] Bracket II item 12 (ordinance p. 145 / PDF p. 149): the printed list places three description lines under number "12." — "Welding shops, machine shops, vehicle repair and repainting shops" (no amount on its own line), "Refrigerator and air conditioning repair shops — 2,500.00", and unnumbered "Furniture shops and sash factories, fabricators (steel, concrete) — 2,500.00". Numbering then resumes at 13. This is a numbering defect in the original; transcribed as printed. All three lines carry Php 2,500.00 rates.

## Sec. 3W.03 — Penalties and Sections
*(ordinance pp. 146-147 / PDF pp. 150-151)*

- **applies_to**: Any violation of this chapter.
- **basis**: Per-violation fines; escalating by offense for entry refusal and anti-pollution-device violations.
- **schedule**:

| Item | Violation | Penalty |
|---|---|---|
| a | Failure to pay the environmental protection and preservation fee | Php. 2,500.00 |
| b | Failure to appoint or designate Pollution Control Officer (PCO) | 2,500.00 |
| c | Refusal to allow CENRO or Task Force Manila Bay (TFMB) Inspectors to ENTER and inspect the business premises — First Offense | P 2,500.00 |
| c | — Second Offense | 5,000.00 |
| c | — Third Offense | 5,000.00 and suspension or revocation of Business Permit |
| d | Failure to install adequate Anti-Pollution Devices — First Offense | Written Warning |
| d | — Second Offense: a. High Risk Industries/Activities | P 5,000.00 |
| d | — Second Offense: b. Industries/Activities that generate Waste or pollution | 5,000.00 |
| d | — Third Offense | Five thousand Pesos (P 5,000.00) and/or Revocation of Business Permit |
| e | Failure to present to or provide CENRO a true copy of all the required clearances, permit, certifications, licenses or similar documents issued by National Government Offices, like but not limited to the DENR-EMB | P 5,000.00 |

- **conditions/notes**:
  - Under (d) First Offense (Written Warning): "A time limit of a minimum of one (1) month or a maximum of twelve (12) months may be given to the violator to comply with the CENRO anti-pollution requirements depending on its magnitude, degree or enormity. Failure to comply with the deadline set by CENRO will result into the imposition of the fines set under the Second Offense rule of this Ordinance stated as follows:" (followed by the Second Offense sub-table above).
  - "In case any violation of this Ordinance is committed by a corporation, association or partnership, any judicial entity, the partner, president, director, manager, trustee, estate administrator, or officer may also be held criminally liable as a co-principal." (as printed; "judicial" sic, likely "juridical")
  - "Penalties must be settled with the Office of the Treasurer within five (5) working days upon receipt of the Ordinance Violation Receipt (OVR)."
- **unclear**:
  - [?] Section heading reads "Penalties and Sections" (PDF p. 150) — as printed; likely intended "Penalties and Sanctions".

## Sec. 3W.04 — Environmental Protection Clearance for New Construction, Renovation, Repair and Alteration Of Existing Structures
*(ordinance p. 147 / PDF p. 151)*

- **applies_to**: Owners applying for building permits for new construction, renovation, repair and/or alteration of existing structures — must first file an application for environmental protection clearance.
- **basis**: Processing fee bracketed by building type/use (residential by dwelling form, multi-story per floor), plus a flat filing fee.
- **schedule**:

**A. Processing Fees**

| Category | Sub-type | Fee (Php) |
|---|---|---|
| 1. Residential Buildings | a. Single Detached | Php 800.00 |
| 1. Residential Buildings | b. Duplex | 1,200.00 |
| 1. Residential Buildings | c. Multi-Story (per floor) | 800.00 |
| 2. Commercial Buildings | — | 1,600.00 |
| 3. Industrial Buildings | a. Warehouse | 1,600.00 |
| 3. Industrial Buildings | b. Manufacturing/Factory | 2,000.00 |
| 4. Institutional Buildings | — | 1,600.00 |

**B. Filing fee**

| Item | Fee (Php) |
|---|---|
| Filing fee | 100.00 |

- **conditions/notes**: "Prior to the processing and approval of building permits for a new construction, renovation, repair and/or alteration of existing structures, the owner thereof shall be required to file an application for environmental protection clearance and the fees shall be in accordance with the following schedule."
- **unclear**: none.

## Sec. 3W.05 — Penalties
*(ordinance pp. 147-148 / PDF pp. 151-152)*

- **applies_to**: "Failure to secure the environmental protection clearance by the owners of residential, industrial, business or agricultural establishments."
- **basis**: Per-violation fines; anti-pollution-device fines bracketed by business type.
- **schedule**:

| Item | Violation | Penalty (Php) |
|---|---|---|
| (b) | Failure to Secure Environmental Protection Clearance | 7,500.00 |
| (c) | Refusal of Entry of Authorized CENRO Inspector | 4,000.00 |
| (d) | Failure to Install Anti-Pollution Devices (if required): 1. Manufacturers/Producers | 7,500.00 |
| (d) | 2. Importers/Exporters | 4,000.00 |
| (d) | 3. Gasoline/LPG Stations/Storage | 7,500.00 |
| (d) | 4. Restaurants/Carinderias/Caterers | 4,000.00 |
| (d) | 5. Services | 3,000.00 |
| (d) | 6. Other Business Activities or Projects | 2,000.00 |

- **conditions/notes**: "If the violator is committed by a firm, partnership, corporation or other juridical entity, the manager, managing partner, trustee, director or person-in-charge with the management of such establishment shall be held liable." (as printed)
- **unclear**:
  - [?] The list begins at item "(b)" — no item "(a)" is printed on ordinance p. 147 (PDF p. 151). Numbering defect in the original; transcribed as printed.
  - Item (d) sub-item 4 is printed "Restaurants/Carinderias/Caterers" on the page image (PDF p. 152); OCR rendered it "Carinderias" as well.

# ARTICLE Z. MAYOR'S PERMIT FEES FOR SIGNS, SIGNBOARDS AND ADVERTISEMENT

## Sec. 3Z.01 — Imposition of Fee
*(ordinance pp. 150-151 / PDF pp. 154-155)*

- **applies_to**: Advertisements (billboards, signs, placards, movie-house slides/films, electric/neon lights, mass sign displays, vehicle/balloon/kite ads, house-to-house promotional sales).
- **basis**: Mixed — per sq.m. or fraction thereof; annual flat fees; per-quarter brackets by display count; per day/week/month; per day per person.
- **schedule**:

| # | Item | Fee (Php) |
|---|---|---|
| 1) | Billboards, signs or advertisements for business and professions, painted on any building or structure or otherwise separated or detached there from, per sq.m. or fraction thereof | 25.00 |
| 2) | Advertisement by means of placards, per sq.m. or fraction thereof | 25.00 |
| 3) | Advertisements for business or profession by means of slides in movie houses or theaters, payable by owners of movie houses, theaters, annually | 300.00 |
| 4) | Advertisements for business or profession by means of film exhibition payable by owners of movie houses or theaters annually | 2,000.00 |
| — | In addition to the fees provided under items 1 and 2 of this Section, for the use of electric or neon lights in billboards, per sq.m. or fraction thereof | 30.00 |
| 5) | Mass display of signs (Per Quarter) — From 100 to 250 displays signs | 500.00 |
| 5) | — From 251 to 500 displays signs | 750.00 |
| 5) | — From 501 to 750 displays signs | 1,000.00 |
| 5) | — From 751 to 1,000 displays signs | 1,500.00 |
| 5) | — For more than 1,000 displays signs | 2,500.00 |
| 6) | Advertisements by means of vehicles, balloons, kites, etc — Per day or fraction thereof | 100.00 |
| 6) | — Per week or fraction thereof | 150.00 |
| 6) | — Per month or fraction thereof | 200.00 |
| 7) | Advertisements by means of promotional sales (house to house) per day, per person | 1,000.00 |

- **conditions/notes**: The electric/neon-light surcharge line (30.00) is printed between items 4 and 5 as an unnumbered rider to items 1 and 2.
- **unclear**:
  - [?] Item 5 bracket layout (ordinance p. 151 / PDF p. 155): the "(Per Quarter)" label and numeral "5)" are printed adjacent to the "Mass display of signs" heading; transcribed as item 5 covering the five display-count brackets.

*(ordinance p. 151 / PDF p. 155)*

## Sec. 4D.02 — Health Certificate Fee — FULL
- **applies_to**: All persons employed in business establishments engaged in food, entertainment and personnel services.
- **basis**: Annual fee; requires medical and physical examination given by the government hospitals, medical clinics, and those private hospitals and medical clinics that may be accredited for the purpose.
- **schedule**: Annual fee of **fifty pesos (Php 50.00)** paid to the City Treasurer; upon favorable results of the medical and health examination and payment, applicant is issued a chronologically numbered and serialized health certificate. City Health Department keeps a file copy of the examination results.
- Source: PDF p-165–166 (ord 161–162).

## Sec. 4D.03 — Time of Payment
- Fees in this Article are paid to the City Treasurer upon filing of the application for the sanitary inspection certificate with the City Health Officer, and upon renewal every year thereafter within the first twenty (20) days [of the year].

## Sec. 4D.04 — Administrative Provisions (summarized)
- (a) The City Health Officer or his duly authorized representative shall conduct annual inspection of all establishments and buildings and accessories and houses for rent, to determine adequacy of ventilation, general sanitary conditions and propriety for habitation, and general sanitary conditions pursuant to existing laws, rules and regulations. Sanitary permit shall be issued to the owner by the City Health Officer or his duly authorized representative after such inspection was conducted and found to be sanitary.
- (b) Every permit issued shall show the name of the applicant, his nationality, marital status, address, nature of organization (whether sole proprietorship, partnership or corporation), location information and other data (as printed).
- (c) The permit shall be granted for a period of not more than one year, expiring on the 31st day of December following the date of issuance thereof, unless revoke or surrendered earlier.
- (d) Every permit shall cease to be in force upon revocation; surrender of same; upon closure of business or discontinuance of the undertaking for which the permit was issued. The business shall be deemed finally closed only upon payment of all taxes, charges and fees due thereon.
- (e) The City Health Officer shall require evidence of payment of the fee imposed herein before he issues the sanitary inspection certification.
- Source: PDF p-166 (ord 162).

| 25 or more but less than 50 sq.ms. | 330.00 |
| Less than 25 sq.ms. | 110.00 |

**5) Independent Wholesalers, Dealers, Distributors, Repackers and Retailers with an aggregate area of:**
| Bracket | Fee (Php) |
|---|---|
| 1,000 sq.ms. or more | 2,200.00 |
| 500 or more but less than 1,000 sq.ms. | 1,540.00 |
| 200 or more but less than 500 sq.ms. | 1,100.00 |

| 50 or more but less than 100 sq.ms. | 330.00 |

| Less than 25 sq.ms. | 110.00 |

### Schedule T – Bulky/Special Waste Collection
Individuals who shall request for the collection of bulky waste and special wastes such as old dilapidated furniture, appliances, construction debris/waste, earthmound, and the like shall be charged in the amount equivalent to the **cost of truck rental per trip**. For proper and effective implementation of Schedule T, the **CENRO** shall be responsible for the issuance of the order of payment to the requesting party before the actual hauling is done.

- **conditions/notes**:
  - **Sec. 4F.02 — Garbage Service Charges for Multiple Business**: where two or more kinds of businesses subject to garbage charges are conducted in the same place or establishment by the same owner or operator, the charge collected is that with the **highest rate** among the businesses concerned **plus twenty five percent (25%)** thereof; provided, that the total garbage fee shall not exceed **Six Thousand pesos (Php 6,000.00) per annum**.
  - **Sec. 4F.03 — Manufacturers/Producers maintaining or operating principal offices, factories and/or sales offices in the same premises**: for collection of garbage charges under Schedule S(1), such manufacturers/producers pay garbage charges based on the **total aggregate area** of the business premises at the rates prescribed under Schedule S(1)(a) [printed "Schedule S (l A)"] of this Article.
  - **Sec. 4F.04 — Time of Payment**: fees in this Article are paid to the City Treasurer **on or before the tenth (10th) day of every month**, or to the authorized representative who shall collect the said fee from the establishment.
- **unclear**:
  - Schedule S(4), second bracket printed as "**00** or more but less than 1,000 sq.ms." (p-174 / ord 170) — image and OCR agree the leading "5" is missing in print; almost certainly **500** by pattern of every other schedule. Marked [?].
  - Schedule A is labeled "PER QUARTER" while Sec. 4F.01 says "annual garbage generation fee" — as printed (p-169 / ord 165); tension is in the source.
  - Schedule G item 3(a)(2): printed "55..00" with doubled period (p-172 / ord 168) — read as 55.00.

# CHAPTER V. CITY CHARGES

## Article A — Rental Fee and Other Charges for Rentable Spaces in the Malabon City Government Center (PDF pp. 187–188, ord. pp. 183–184)

### Sec. 5A.01 — Collection of Rental Fees and Charges
- **applies_to**: Rentable space within the City Government Center of Malabon (lessees of office/commercial space).
- **basis**: Per square meter, per month.
- **schedule**:

| Item | Rate |
|---|---|
| Rentable space within the City Government Center of Malabon | at least Two Hundred Pesos (P200.00) per square meter per month |

- **conditions/notes**: "shall charge at least" — ₱200.00/sqm/month is a minimum.

### Sec. 5A.02 — Rate Adjustment
- The Finance Committee is authorized to increase the minimum fees and rates not less than ten percent (10%) every three (3) years starting 2009.

### Sec. 5A.03 — Lease of Office Space
- City Mayor authorized to enter and negotiate lease contracts for periods not exceeding five (5) years, for areas determined not to be needed for use by the City.
- If a leased space becomes needed by the City: General Services Department recommends to the City Mayor which areas to withdraw from commercial lease; withdrawal takes effect after expiration of the existing lease contract. Withdrawal of an *unoccupied* area previously designated for lease takes effect immediately upon City Mayor's approval. (Continuation paragraph at top of PDF p. 188.)

### Sec. 5A.04 — Collection of Rental Fees and Charges
- Verbatim: "The City Government of Malabon shall charge at least Two Hundred Pesos (P200.00)"
- **unclear**: [?] The printed sentence ends at "(P200.00)" with no continuation on the page (PDF p. 188) — appears to be a truncated duplicate of Sec. 5A.01 in the original ordinance. Confirmed against both image and OCR.

### Sec. 5A.05 — Time of Payment
- The fee or charge shall be paid to the City Treasurer or his duly deputized representative.

## Article D — Lease of Commercial Spaces of Real Properties Owned by the City Government (PDF pp. 191–192, ord. pp. 187–188)

### Sec. 5D.01 — Authority to Lease
- **applies_to**: Any person, corporation or firm leasing real properties owned by the City Government of Malabon — residential, commercial, and/or industrial.
- **conditions/notes**: City Mayor authorized to negotiate/enter lease agreements for periods not to exceed five (5) years, renewable at the local chief executive's discretion. Same withdrawal mechanics as Art. A: GSD recommendation; effective after expiration of existing lease agreement; immediate for unoccupied designated areas upon Mayor's approval.

### Sec. 5D.02 — Rates
- **basis**: Per square meter, per month, in Philippine currency.
- **schedule**:

| Item | Rate |
|---|---|
| Lease of commercial spaces of real properties owned by the City Government | Two Hundred Pesos (Php200.00) / square meter per month |

- **conditions/notes**: Excluding utilities such as water, electricity and any other improvements to be made on the property by the lessee.

### Sec. 5D.03 — Rate Adjustments
- The Finance Committee is authorized to increase the minimum fees and rates not less than ten percent (10%) every two (2) years.

### Sec. 5D.04 — Issuance of Receipt
- Official receipt shall be issued upon payment of rental to the City Treasury Department, which the City General Services Department shall record for record purposes and future reference (sentence continues onto PDF p. 192).

# CHAPTER VII — COMMUNITY TAX
*(PDF pp. 209–212 / ord. pp. 205–208)*

## Sec. 7.01 — Imposition of Tax
- **rule**: "There shall be imposed a community tax on persons, natural or juridical, residing in the city."
- **notes**: None.

## Sec. 7.02 — Individuals Liable to Community Tax
- **rule**: Every inhabitant of the Philippines who is a resident of this city, eighteen (18) years of age or over who has been regularly employed on a wage or salary basis for at least thirty (30) consecutive working days during any calendar year, or who is engaged in business or occupation, or who owns real property with an aggregate assessed value of **One Thousand (₱1,000.00) Pesos or more**, or who is required by law to file an income tax return shall pay:
  - **Basic annual community tax: Five (₱5.00) Pesos**; and
  - **Additional annual tax: One Peso (₱1.00) for every One Thousand Pesos (₱1,000.00) of income** regardless of whether from business, exercise of profession or from property, **which in no case shall exceed Five Thousand Pesos (₱5,000.00)**.

  In the case of husband and wife, the additional tax herein imposed shall be based upon the total property owned by them and the total gross receipts or earnings derived by them.
- **notes**: OCR renders "engaged in business or corporation"; the image supports the standard LGC Sec. 157 phrase "business or occupation" — the word is smudged. [?] PDF p. 209 (minor).

## Sec. 7.03 — Juridical Persons Liable to Community Tax
- **rule**: Every corporation no matter how created or organized, whether domestic or resident-foreign, engaged in or doing business in the Philippines whose principal office is located in this city shall pay:
  - **Basic annual Community Tax: Five Hundred Pesos (₱500.00)**; and
  - **Additional tax, which in no case shall exceed Ten Thousand Pesos (₱10,000.00)**, in accordance with the following schedule:
- **schedule** (PDF p. 210):
  - (a) For every Five Thousand (₱5,000.00) Pesos worth of real property in the Philippines owned by it during the preceding year based on the valuation used in the payment of real property tax under existing laws, found in the assessment rolls of this city where the real property is situated — **Two (₱2.00) Pesos**; and
  - (b) For every Five Thousand (₱5,000.00) Pesos of gross receipts or earnings derived by it from its business in the Philippines during the preceding year — **Two (₱2.00) Pesos**.

  The dividends received by a corporation from another corporation shall, for the purpose of the additional tax, be considered as part of the gross receipts or earnings of said corporation.

## Sec. 7.04 — Exemption
- **rule**: The following are exempted from the Community Tax:
  - (a) Diplomatic and consular representatives; and
  - (b) Transient visitors when their stay in the Philippines does not exceed three (3) months.

## Sec. 7.05 — [no title printed; content = Time for Payment / Accrual; Penalties for Delinquency]
- **rule** (PDF pp. 210–211):
  - (a) "The Community Tax shall accrue to the first (1st) day of January each year which shall be paid not later than the date of February of each year." *(sic — see notes)*
  - (b) If a person reaches the age of eighteen (18) years or otherwise loses the benefit of exemption on or before the last day of June, he shall be liable for the payment of community tax on the day he reached such age or upon the day the exemption ends. If a person reaches the age of eighteen (18) years or loses the benefit of exemption on or before the last day of March, he shall have twenty (20) days within which to pay the community tax without being delinquent.
  - (c) Persons who come to reside in the Philippines or reach the age of eighteen (18) years on or after the first (1st) day of July of any year, or who cease to belong to an exempt class on or after the same date, shall not be subject to community tax for that year.
  - (d) Corporations established and organized on or before the last day of June shall be liable for the payment of community tax for that year. Corporations established or organized on or before the last day of March shall have twenty (20) days within which to pay the community tax without becoming delinquent. Corporations established and organized on or after the first day of July shall not be subject to community tax for that year.
  - (e) **If the tax is not paid within the time prescribed above, there shall be added to the unpaid amount an interest of twenty-four percent (24%) per annum from the due date until it is paid.**
- **notes**: The section heading is printed simply "Section 7.05.-" with no title (confirmed on image and OCR). Sub-(a) as printed says "accrue **to** the first (1st) day of January … paid not later than **the date of February**"; the LGC Sec. 161 source text reads "accrue **on** the first day of January … paid not later than the **last day** of February." Transcribed verbatim; the ordinance print appears to drop "last day". [?] PDF pp. 210–211.
- **unclear**: [?] PDF p. 210 — Sec. 7.05(a) "the date of February" (likely misprint for "the last day of February"); flagged, not corrected.

## Sec. 7.06 — Community Tax Certificate
- **rule**: A Community Tax Certificate shall be issued to every person or corporation upon payment of the Community Tax. A Community Tax Certificate may also be issued to any person or corporation not subject to the Community Tax upon payment of **One Peso (₱1.00)**.

## Sec. 7.07 — Presentation of Community Tax Certificate on Certain Occasions
- **rule** (PDF p. 211):
  - (a) When an individual subject to community tax acknowledges any document before a notary public, takes oath of office upon election or appointment to any position in the government service, receives any license, certificate, or permit from any public authority; pays any tax or fee; receives any money from any public fund; transacts other official business, or receives any salary or wage from any person or corporation, it shall be the duty of any person, officer, or corporation with whom such transaction is made or business done or from whom any salary or wage is received to require such individual to exhibit the community tax certificate.

    The presentation of community tax shall not be required in connection with the registration of a voter.
  - (b) When through its authorized officers, any corporation subject to community tax receives any license, certificate, or permit from any public authority, pay any tax or fee, receives money from public funds, or transacts other official business, it shall be the duty of the public official with whom such transaction is made or business done, to require such corporation to exhibit the community tax certificate.
  - (c) The community tax certificate required in the two preceding paragraphs shall be the one issued for the current year, except for the period of January until the fifteenth (15th) of April each year, in which case, the certificate issued for the preceding year shall suffice.
- **notes**: Directly relevant to permit processing — the CTC (cedula) must be exhibited when receiving any license/permit or paying any tax/fee; current-year certificate required, except Jan 1–Apr 15 when the preceding year's certificate suffices.

## Sec. 7.08 — Collection and Allocation of Proceeds of the Community Tax
- **rule** (PDF p. 212):
  - (a) The City Treasurer shall deputize the Barangay Treasurer, subject to existing laws and regulation, to collect the Community Tax payable by individual taxpayers in their respective jurisdiction; provided, however, that said Barangay Treasurer shall be bonded in accordance with existing laws;
  - (b) One Hundred Percent (100%) of the proceeds of the Community Tax actually and directly collected by the City Treasurer shall accrue entirely to the general fund of the city.

    The proceeds of the Community Tax collected through the Barangay Treasurer shall be apportioned as follows:
    - (1) Fifty percent (50%) shall accrue to the general fund of the city; and
    - (2) Fifty percent (50%) shall accrue to the barangay where the tax is collected.

---

# CHAPTER VIII — GENERAL ADMINISTRATIVE PROVISIONS
*(PDF pp. 212–222 / ord. pp. 208–218)*

## Article A. Collection and Accounting of City Taxes and Other Impositions

## Sec. 8A.01 — Tax Period
- **rule**: Unless otherwise provided in this Ordinance, the tax period for all local taxes, fees, and charges imposed under this Ordinance shall be the calendar year.

## Sec. 8A.02 — Accrual of Tax
- **rule**: Unless otherwise provided in this Ordinance, all taxes and charges imposed herein shall accrue on the first (1st) day of January of each year. However, new taxes, fees or charges, or changes in the rate of existing taxes, fees, or charges, shall accrue on the first (1st) day of the quarter next following the effectivity of the Ordinance imposing such new levies or taxes.

## Sec. 8A.03 — Time of Payment
- **rule**: Unless specifically provided herein, all taxes, fees, and charges imposed in this Ordinance shall be paid within the first twenty (20) days of January or each subsequent quarter as the case may be.

## Sec. 8A.04 — Surcharge for Late Payment
- **rule**: "Failure to pay the tax described in this Article within the time required shall subject the taxpayer to a **surcharge of twenty-five percent (25%) of the original amount of tax due**, such surcharge to be paid at the same time and in the same manner as the tax due." (spans PDF pp. 212–213)

## Sec. 8A.05 — Interest on Unpaid Tax
- **rule** (PDF p. 213): "In addition to the surcharge imposed herein, where the amount of any other revenue due to the city except voluntary contributions or donations, is not paid on the date fixed in the ordinance, or in the contract, expressed or implied, or upon the occurrence of the event which has given rise to its collection, there shall be collected as part of that amount an **interest at the rate not to exceed two percent (2%) per month** from the date it is due until it is paid, **but in no case shall the total interest on the unpaid amount or a portion thereof exceed thirty-six (36) months**.

  Where an extension of time for the payment of the tax has been granted and the amount is not paid in full prior to the expiration of the extension, the interest above-mentioned shall be collected on the unpaid amount from the date it becomes originally due until fully paid."
- **notes**: Combined delinquency charge for the system: 25% surcharge (one-time, Sec. 8A.04) + up to 2%/month interest capped at 36 months (max 72%).

## Sec. 8A.06 — Collection
- **rule**: Unless otherwise specified, all taxes, fees and charges due to this city shall be collected by the City Treasurer or his duly authorized representatives. Unless otherwise specifically provided in this Ordinance or under existing laws and ordinances, the City Treasurer is hereby authorized, subject to the approval of the City Mayor, to promulgate rules and regulations for the proper and efficient administration and collection of taxes, fees and charges herein levied and imposed.

## Sec. 8A.07 — Issuance of Receipts
- **rule**: It shall be the duty of the City Treasurer or his authorized representative to issue the required official receipt to the person paying the tax, fee or charge wherein the date, amount, name of the person paying and the account for which it is paid, are shown. The Ordinance Number and the specific section thereof upon which collections are based shall invariably be indicated on the face of all official receipts acknowledging payment of taxes, fees, or charges.

## Sec. 8A.08 — Record of Persons Paying Revenue
- **rule**: It shall be the duty of the City Treasurer to keep a record, alphabetically arranged and open to public inspection during office hours, of the names of all persons paying city taxes, fees and charges. He shall, as far as practicable, establish and keep current the appropriate tax roll for each kind of tax, fee or charge provided in this Ordinance.

## Sec. 8A.09 — Accounting of Collections
- **rule**: Unless otherwise provided in this Ordinance and other existing laws and ordinances, all monies collected by virtue of this Ordinance shall be accounted for in accordance with the provisions of existing laws, rules and regulations and credited to the General Fund of the City. (spans PDF pp. 213–214)

## Sec. 8A.10 — Examination of Books of Accounts
- **rule** (PDF p. 214): The City Treasurer shall, by himself or through any of his deputies duly authorized in writing, examine the books of accounts and other pertinent records of the business establishments doing business within the city, and subject to city taxes, to ascertain, assess and collect the true and correct amount of the tax due from the taxpayer concerned. Such examination shall be made during regular business hours **once every year for every tax period**, which shall be the year immediately preceding the examination. Any examination conducted pursuant to the provisions of this Section shall be certified to by the examining official and such certificate shall be made of record in the books of accounts of the taxpayer concerned.

  In case the examination herein authorized is to be made by a duly authorized deputy of the City Treasurer, there shall be written authority issued to the former which shall specifically state the name, address and business of the taxpayer whose books of accounts and pertinent records are to be examined, the date and place of such examination, and the procedure to be followed in conducting the same.

  For this purpose, the records of the Revenue District Office of the Bureau of Internal Revenue shall be made available to the City Treasurer, his deputy or duly authorized representative.

  The forms and the guidelines to be observed for the proper and effective implementation of this Section shall be those prescribed by the Department of Finance.

## Sec. 8A.11 — Accrual to the General Fund of Fines, Costs, and Forfeitures
- **rule**: Unless otherwise provided by law or ordinance, fines, costs, forfeitures, and other pecuniary liabilities imposed by the court for violation of any city ordinance shall accrue to the General Fund of the city.

## Article B. Civil Remedies for Collection of Revenues

## Sec. 8B.01 — Local Government's Lien
- **rule** (PDF pp. 214–215): Local taxes, fees, charges and other revenues herein provide constitute a lien, superior to all liens, charges or encumbrances in favor of any person, enforceable by appropriate administrative or judicial action, not only upon any property or rights therein which may be subject to lien but upon also property used in business, occupation, practice of profession or calling, or exercise of privilege with respect to which the lien is imposed. The lien may only be extinguished upon full payment of the delinquent local taxes, fees, and charges including related surcharges and interest.

## Sec. 8B.02 — Civil Remedies
- **rule** (PDF p. 215): The civil remedies for the collection of local business taxes, fees, or charges, and related surcharges and interest resulting from delinquency shall be:
  - (a) By administrative action through distraint of goods, chattels or effects, and other personal property of whatever character, including stocks and other securities, debts, credits, bank accounts, and interest in and rights to personal property, and to levy upon real property and interest in or rights to real property; and
  - (b) By judicial action.

  Either of these remedies or all may be pursued concurrently or simultaneously at the discretion of the City Treasurer.

## Sec. 8B.03 — Distraint of Personal Property (and Levy on Real Property)
- **rule** (PDF pp. 215–220, items a–p; key deadlines and amounts):
  - a) *Seizure.* Upon failure to pay at the time required, the City Treasurer or his deputy may, upon written notice, seize or confiscate personal property of the delinquent (or property subject to the lien) sufficient to satisfy the tax, fee or charge plus delinquency increments and expenses of seizure; a duly authenticated certificate of delinquency serves as sufficient warrant for distraint, subject to the taxpayer's right to claim exemption under existing laws. Distrained property is sold at public auction as herein provided.
  - b) *Accounting of Distrained Goods.* The officer executing the distraint shall make an account of the goods distrained, a signed copy left with the owner/possessor or at his dwelling/place of business with someone of suitable age and discretion, with a statement of the sum demanded and a note of the time and place of sale.
  - c) *Publication.* Notification exhibited in not less than three (3) conspicuous places in the territory of the local government unit where the distraint is made, specifying the time and place of sale and the articles distrained. The time of sale shall not be less than twenty (20) days after notice to the owner or possessor and the publication or posting of the notice. One posting place shall be the Office of the City Mayor. (spans pp. 215–216)
  - d) *Release of Distrained Property Upon Payment Prior to Sale.* "If not any time prior to the consummation of the sale, all proper charges are paid to the officer conducting the same, the goods or effects distrained shall be restored to the owner." *(sic — "If not any time" is as printed; evidently "If at any time")* [?] PDF p. 216
  - e) *Procedure of Sale.* Sale at public auction to the highest bidder for cash at the time and place fixed in the notice. Within five (5) days after the same [sale], the City Treasurer shall make a written report of the proceedings to the City Mayor. Should the property distrained not be disposed of within one hundred and twenty (120) days from the date of distraint, it shall be considered as sold to the local government unit concerned for the amount of the assessment made by the Committee on Appraisal, and to the extent of the same amount the tax delinquencies shall be canceled. The Committee on Appraisal: City Treasurer as Chairman, with a representative of the Commission on Audit and the City Assessor as Members.
  - f) *Disposition of Proceeds.* Proceeds applied to the tax including surcharges, interest, and other penalties incident to delinquency, and expenses of distraint and sale; balance returned to the owner. Expenses chargeable cover only actual expenses of seizure and preservation pending sale; no charge for the services of the local officer. If proceeds are insufficient, other property may in like manner be distrained until the full amount due, including all expenses, is collected.
  - g) *Levy on Real Property.* After expiration of the time required to pay, real property may be levied on before, simultaneously or after distraint of personal property. The City Treasurer prepares a duly authenticated certificate (name of taxpayer, amount of tax/fee/charge and penalty due) operating with the force of a legal execution throughout the Philippines. Levy is effected by writing upon said certificate the description of the property levied; written notice of the levy is mailed to or served upon the Assessor and Register of Deeds of the city (who annotate the levy on the tax declaration and certificate of title), and on the delinquent taxpayer or his agent/manager, or the occupant of the property. If levy is not issued before or simultaneously with the warrant of distraint, and personal property is insufficient, the City Treasurer shall within thirty (30) days after execution of the distraint proceed with the levy on the taxpayer's real property. A report on any levy shall, within ten (10) days after receipt of the warrant, be submitted by the levying officer to the Sangguniang Panlungsod. (spans pp. 216–217)
  - h) *Penalty for Failure to Issue and Execute Warrant.* Without prejudice to criminal prosecution under the Revised Penal Code and other applicable laws, the City Treasurer, if he fails to issue or execute the warrant of distraint or levy after the expiration of the time prescribed, or if found guilty of abusing the exercise thereof by competent authority, shall be automatically dismissed from the service after due notice and hearing.
  - i) *Advertisement and Sale.* Within thirty (30) days after levy, the City Treasurer shall publicly advertise for sale or auction the property or a usable portion thereof; advertisement covers a period of at least thirty (30) days — by posting a notice at the main entrance of the city hall and in a public and conspicuous place in the barangay where the real property is located, and by publication once a week for three (3) weeks in a newspaper of general circulation in the city. The advertisement shall state the amount of taxes, fees or charges and penalties due, time and place of sale, name of the taxpayer, and a short description of the property. At any time before the date fixed for the sale, the taxpayer may stay the proceedings by paying the taxes, fees, charges, penalties and interests. Otherwise the sale proceeds at the main entrance of the City Hall, or on the property to be sold, or any other place determined by the City Treasurer and specified in the notice of sale. Within thirty (30) days after the sale, the City Treasurer or his deputy shall make a report of the sale to the Sangguniang Panlungsod, which shall form part of his records; after consultation with the Sanggunian, the City Treasurer delivers to the purchaser a certificate of sale; any excess of proceeds over the claim and cost of sale is turned over to the owner. The City Treasurer may, by a duly approved ordinance, advance an amount sufficient to defray the costs of collection. (spans pp. 217–218)
  - j) *Redemption of Property Sold.* Within one (1) year from the date of sale, the delinquent taxpayer or his representative may redeem the property upon payment to the City Treasurer of the total amount of taxes, fees or charges, and related surcharges, interests or penalties from the date of delinquency to the date of sale, **plus interest of not more than two percent (2%) per month on the purchase price** from the date of purchase to the date of redemption. Such payment invalidates the certificate of sale, and the owner is entitled to a certificate of redemption. The City Treasurer, upon surrender by the purchaser of the certificate of sale, returns to the purchaser the entire purchase price plus interest of not more than two percent (2%) per month, the portion of the cost of sale and other legitimate expenses. The owner is not deprived of possession and remains entitled to rentals and other income until expiration of the redemption period. (PDF p. 218)
  - k) *Final Deed of Purchaser.* In case the taxpayer fails to redeem the property as provided herein, the City Treasurer shall execute a deed conveying to the purchaser so much of the property as has been sold, free from liens of any taxes, fees, charges, related surcharges, interests and penalties. The deed shall sufficiently recite all the proceedings upon which the validity of the sale depends. (PDF p. 218, bottom)
  - l) *Purchase of Property by the City for Want of Bidder.* If there is no bidder, or the highest bid is insufficient to pay the taxes, fees, or charges, related surcharges, interests, penalties and cost, the City Treasurer shall purchase the property on behalf of the city to satisfy the claim, and within two (2) days thereafter shall make a report of his proceedings, to be reflected upon the records of his office. The Registrar of Deeds concerned shall, upon registration of any such declaration of forfeiture, transfer the title of the forfeited property to the city without the necessity of a court order. Within one (1) year from the date of such forfeiture, the taxpayer or any of his representatives may redeem the property by paying the City Treasurer the full amount of the taxes, fees, charges and related surcharges, interests or penalties, and the costs of sale; if not redeemed, ownership shall be fully vested in the city. (PDF p. 219)
  - m) *Resale of Real Estate Taken for Taxes, Fees or Charges.* The Sangguniang Panlungsod may, by a duly approved ordinance, and upon notice of not less than twenty (20) days, sell and dispose of the real property acquired under the preceding paragraph at public auction; proceeds accrue to the general fund of the city.
  - n) *Collection of Delinquent Taxes, Fees, Charges or Other Revenues Through Judicial Action.* The city may enforce collection by civil action in any court of competent jurisdiction, filed by the City Treasurer within the period prescribed in Section 194 of Republic Act No. 7160, as implemented under Article 284 of the Implementing Rules and Regulations (IRR).
  - o) *Further Distraint or Levy.* The remedies by distraint and levy may be repeated if necessary until the full amount due, including all expenses, is collected.
  - p) *Personal Property Exempt from Distraint of Levy.* Exempt from distraint and the levy, attachment or execution for delinquency in the payment of any local tax, fee or charge, including the related surcharge and interest:
    1. Tools and the implements necessarily used by the delinquent taxpayer in the trade or employment;
    2. One (1) horse, cow, carabao, or other beast of burden, such as the delinquent taxpayer may select, and necessarily used by him in his occupation;
    3. His necessary clothing, and that of all his family;
    4. Household furniture and utensils necessary for housekeeping and used for that purpose by the delinquent taxpayer, such as he may select, of a value not exceeding Ten Thousand Pesos (₱10,000.00);
    5. Provisions, including crops, actually provided for individual or family use sufficient for four (4) months;
    6. The professional libraries of doctors, engineers, lawyers and judges;
    7. One fishing boat and net, not exceeding the total value of Ten Thousand Pesos (₱10,000.00), by the lawful use of which a fisherman earns his livelihood; and
    8. Any material or article forming part of a house or improvement of any real property.
- **unclear**:
  - [?] PDF p. 216, item d): "If not any time prior…" as printed (apparent misprint for "If at any time prior").
  - [?] PDF p. 219, first item letter appears as "l)" (OCR reads "1)"); sequence a)–p) with k) at the bottom of PDF p. 218 confirms it is "l)".
  - [?] PDF p. 219: heading printed "Resale of Real Estate Take for Taxes…" (OCR) — "Taken" intended.

## Article C. Taxpayer's Remedies

## Sec. 8C.01 — Periods of Assessment and Collection
- **rule** (PDF pp. 220–221):
  - (a) Local taxes, fees, or charges shall be **assessed within five (5) years from the date they became due**. No action for collection, whether administrative or judicial, shall be instituted after the expiration of such period: Provided, that taxes, fees, or charges which have accrued before the effectivity of the Local Government Code of 1991 may be assessed within a period of five (5) years from the date they became due.
  - (b) In case of **fraud or intent to evade** payment, the same may be assessed within **ten (10) years from discovery** of the fraud or intent to evade payment.
  - (c) Local taxes, fees, or charges may be **collected within five (5) years from the date of assessment** by administrative or judicial action. No such action shall be instituted after the expiration of said period: Provided, however, that taxes, fees and charges assessed before the effectivity of the LGC of 1991 may be assessed within a period of three (3) years from the date of assessment. *(sic — "assessed" printed where LGC Sec. 194(c) says "collected")*
  - (d) The running of the periods of prescription shall be suspended for the time during which:
    1. The treasurer is legally prevented from making the assessment of collection; *(sic — likely "or collection")*
    2. The taxpayer requests for a reinvestigation and executes a waiver in writing before expiration of the period within which to assess or collect; and
    3. The taxpayer is out of the country or otherwise cannot be located.
- **unclear**: [?] PDF p. 220 (c): "may be assessed within a period of three (3) years" as printed; [?] PDF p. 221 (d)(1): "assessment of collection" as printed.

## Sec. 8C.02 — Protest of Assessment
- **rule** (PDF p. 221): When the City Treasurer or his duly authorized representative finds that correct taxes, fees, or charges have not been paid, he shall issue a **notice of assessment** stating the nature of the tax, fee or charge, the amount of deficiency, the surcharges, interests and penalties.

  **Within sixty (60) days from receipt of the notice of assessment**, the taxpayer may file a **written protest** with the City Treasurer contesting the assessment; otherwise, the assessment shall become final and executory. The City Treasurer shall **decide the protest within sixty (60) days** from the time of its filing. In cases where the protest is denied, the taxpayer shall have **thirty (30) days from receipt of the denial or from the lapse of the sixty-day period** within which to **appeal with the court of competent jurisdiction**; otherwise the assessment becomes conclusive and unappealable.

## Sec. 8C.03 — Claim for Refund of tax Credit *(title as printed)*
- **rule**: No case or proceeding shall be maintained in any court for the recovery of any tax, fee, or charge erroneously or illegally collected until a **written claim for refund or credit has been filed with the City Treasurer**. No case or proceeding shall be entertained in any court after the expiration of **two (2) years from the date of payment** of such tax, fee or charge, **or from the date the taxpayer is entitled to a refund or credit**.
- **notes**: Title as printed reads "Refund of tax Credit" (likely "Refund or Tax Credit").

## Sec. 8C.04 — Legality of this Code
- **rule**: Any question on the constitutionality or legality of this Ordinance may be raised on **appeal within thirty (30) days from the effectivity thereof to the Secretary of Justice**, who shall render a decision within **sixty (60) days** from receipt of the appeal: Provided, however, that such appeal shall not have the effect of suspending the effectivity of this Ordinance and the accrual and payment of the tax, fee or charge levied herein: Provided finally, that within **thirty (30) days after receipt of the decision or the lapse of the sixty-day period** without the Secretary of Justice acting upon the appeal, the aggrieved party may file the appropriate proceedings with a court of competent jurisdiction.

## Article D. Miscellaneous Provisions

## Sec. 8D.01 — Power to Levy Other Taxes, Fees or Charges
- **rule** (PDF p. 222): The city may exercise the power to levy taxes, fees or charges on any base or subject not otherwise specifically enumerated herein or taxed under the provisions of the National Internal Revenue Code, as amended, or other applicable laws: Provided, that the taxes, fees or charges shall not be unjust, excessive, oppressive, confiscatory or contradictory to declared national policy: Provided, further, that the ordinance levying such taxes, fees or charges shall not be enacted without any prior public hearing conducted for the purpose.

## Sec. 8D.02 — Publication of the Revenue Code
- **rule**: Within ten (10) days after its approval, a certified copy of this Ordinance shall be published in full for three (3) consecutive days in a newspaper of local circulation. Provided, however, that in cases where there are no newspapers of local circulation, the same may be posted in at least two (2) conspicuous and publicly accessible places.

## Sec. 8D.03 — Public Dissemination of this Code
- **rule**: Copies of this Revenue Code shall be furnished to the City Treasurer for public dissemination.

## Sec. 8D.04 — Authority to Adjust Rates
- **rule**: The Sangguniang Panlungsod shall have the sole authority to adjust tax rates as prescribed herein **not oftener than once every five (5) years**, but in no case shall such adjustment **exceed ten percent (10%)** of the rates fixed under this Code.

## Sec. 8D.05 — Withdrawal of Tax Exemption Privileges
- **rule**: Unless otherwise provided in this Revenue Code, tax exemptions or incentives granted to, or presently enjoyed by all persons, whether natural or juridical, including government-owned or controlled corporations, are hereby withdrawn — **except**: local water districts; cooperatives duly registered under RA 6938; non-stock and non-profit hospitals and educational institutions; business enterprises certified by the Board of Investment (BOI) as pioneer or non-pioneer for a period of six (6) and four (4) years, respectively, from the date of registration; business entity, association, or cooperatives registered under RA 6810; and printers and/or publishers of books or other reading materials prescribed by DECS as school texts or references, insofar as receipts from the printing and/or publishing thereof are concerned.

# CHAPTER IX — GENERAL PENAL PROVISIONS
*(PDF pp. 222–223 / ord. pp. 218–219)*

## Sec. 9.01 — Penalties for Violation of Tax Ordinance
- **rule**: "Any person or persons who violates any of the provisions of this Ordinance or the rules or regulations promulgated by authority of this Ordinance shall, upon conviction, be punished by a **fine of not less than One Thousand Pesos (₱1,000.00) nor more than Five Thousand Pesos (₱5,000.00)**, or **imprisonment of not less than one (1) month nor more than six (6) months, or both**, at the discretion of the court.

  If the violation is committee *(sic — "committed")* by any juridical entity, the President, General Manager, or the individual entrusted with the administration thereof at the time of the commission of the violation shall be held responsible or liable therefor.

  Punishment by a fine or imprisonment as herein provided for shall not relieve the offender from the payment of the tax, fee or charge imposed under this Ordinance."
- **unclear**: [?] PDF p. 223: "committee" printed for "committed" — transcribed verbatim.

# CHAPTER X — FINAL PROVISIONS
*(PDF p. 223 / ord. p. 219)*

## Sec. 10.01 — Separability Clause
- **rule**: If for any reason, any section or provision of this Ordinance shall be held to be unconstitutional or invalid by competent authority, such judgment or action shall not affect or impair the other sections or provisions thereof.

## Sec. 10.02 — Applicability Clause
- **rule**: All other matters relating to the impositions in this Ordinance shall be governed by pertinent provisions of existing laws and other ordinances.

## Sec. 10.03 — Repealing Clause
- **rule**: Municipal Ordinance No. 30-1993 and City Ordinance No. 16-2005 are hereby repealed. All ordinances, rules and regulations, or part thereof, in conflict with, or inconsistent with any provisions of this Ordinance are hereby also repealed or modified accordingly.

## Sec. 10A.04 — Effectivity *(numbered "10A.04" as printed; evidently 10.04)*
- **rule**: This Ordinance shall take effect after three (3) consecutive publication and posting in two (2) conspicuous places in the City.

  "Enacted, ______" *(enactment date line blank/illegible on this page)*
- **unclear**: [?] PDF p. 223: section number printed "10A.04" (inconsistent with 10.01–10.03); enactment date not legible on the scanned page.

# Appendix A — Disclosure table of source defects

Every `[sic]`, `[?]`, and anomaly flagged across the ten extracts, consolidated. Pages are **PDF pages** (ordinance page = PDF page − 4). Treatment legend: **verbatim** = transcribed exactly as printed; **certified copy** = needs confirmation against a certified copy of the ordinance before being relied on; **national ref** = the corresponding national-source figure/wording (DPWH D.O. 155 s. 1992, LGC/RA 7160, or other statute) is noted for comparison but was not substituted.

| # | Page (PDF) | Section | What is printed | Why suspect | Treatment |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 34, 36 | 2E.03 / 2F.05 | "…following each quarter In the case of…" | Period after "quarter" faint/absent in print | Verbatim; sense unambiguous |
| 2 | 35 | 2F.03 | "within this province, excluding the territorial limits of the city" | Drafting carry-over from LGC Sec. 137 (provincial franchise tax); read literally it excludes city receipts, contradicting the article | Verbatim; certified copy + legal review before hard-coding territorial scope; national ref |
| 3 | 35 | 2F.03 | "a tax on business enjoying a franchise tax" | Trailing "tax" apparent typo for "a franchise" | Verbatim |
| 4 | 36–37 | 2G.06 | "…authorized to practice his profession in this province…" | "province" carry-over in a city ordinance | Verbatim |

# Appendix B — Data the application must capture

Derived from the fee bases across all extracted articles. Each field is an input the assessment engine needs to compute at least one imposition in this document.

| Field | Drives | Source sections |
|---|---|---|
| **Capitalization / capital investment** (new business) | New-business tax in lieu of gross receipts (0.05% of capital) for printing/publication and franchise; sworn statement of capital before start of operations; branch/sales-outlet permit-fee bracket (over 10M / 1M–10M / below 1M); CENRO fee threshold (min. capitalization Php100,000) | 2E.01, 2F.03, 2N.04(d), 3A.03 branch-fee table, 3W.01 |
| **Gross sales/receipts, preceding calendar year** (renewal) | All graduated business-tax schedules; percentage taxes (printing 0.75%, franchise 0.75%, banks 0.75%, mobile traders 1%, retailers 3%/1.5%); declaration of previous year's gross sales at renewal; ITR reconciliation and deficiency-tax computation; PIL validation floor | 2J.02(a)–(h), 2E.01, 2F.03, 2K.02, 2N.04(d)(e), 2O.01–.03, 3A.05(b)(2)(g) |
| **Line(s) of business + category mapping** | Selection of the correct 2J.02 schedule (a)–(h); combined vs. separate computation for multiple lines (same rate → combine gross; different rates → separate); Mayor's Permit fee category (3A.03 items I–IX, 64 service types); sanitary fee category; garbage schedule (A–S); CENRO bracket (high-risk vs. waste-generating); separate permit per business line | 2J.02, 2N.01, 3A.03, 4D.01, 4F.01–.02, 3W.02 |
| **Floor area (sqm)** | Zoning processing fee (per sqm by use); Mayor's Permit fee for parking areas, warehouses/bodegas, offices (item 64 brackets); building-permit construction fees (per-sqm by Division); occupancy certificate for Division J-1; annual inspection fees; sanitary fee catch-all brackets; garbage fee brackets (factories, eateries, services, wholesalers/retailers, gasoline stations, terminals); city-space lease (per sqm/month) | 3.D.01(c), 3A.03 items 15/17/64, 3V.01 items 3/10/11, 4D.01(m), 4F.01 Schedules E/N/S, 5A.01/5D.02 |
| **Number of employees / food handlers / personal-service workers** | Health certificate (Php50/person/year) for all workers in food, entertainment and personal services; Food Handler's Certificate (renewable every 6 months); medical certificates for masseurs/barbers/beauticians; certified list of employed professionals (professional-tax compliance) | 4D.02, 3A.06(a)(c), 2G.08(c) |
| **Delivery vehicles count** | Annual fixed tax P750.00 per truck/van/motor vehicle (P100.00 tier as printed — see Appendix A #6); Mayor's sticker per vehicle | 2I.01–2I.04 |
| **Storeys / doors / units / rooms / beds** (where applicable) | Real-estate lessor permit fees (storeys for buildings, doors for apartments); sanitary fees (per room for hotels/motels, per door for apartments, boarder-count brackets for dormitories); garbage fees (per room/bed by hotel class, condominium unit brackets, hospital bed-capacity brackets, theater seating brackets, school enrollment brackets) | 3A.03 item 62, 4D.01(e), 4F.01 Schedules B/F/G/H |
| **Goods class, for manufacturers/importers/exporters** | Four-column Mayor's Permit fee matrix: (1) flammable/combustible/explosive substances; (2) non-flammable chemicals/substances; (3) assorted dry goods/merchandise; (4) consumable/perishable/refrigerated goods | 3A.03 items I–III |
| **Signage presence / size / type / count** | Sign fees under building schedule (erection per sqm; installation and annual renewal per sqm by type — neon/illuminated/others/painted-on, business vs. advertising, with minimums); Mayor's Permit sign fees (billboards/placards P25/sqm, neon surcharge P30/sqm, mass-display quarterly brackets by count, per-day vehicle/balloon ads) | 3V.01 item 9.h, 3Z.01 |
| **Liquor / tobacco sale flags** | Liquor filing fee by class (retail/wholesale/serving/manufacture/amusement); prohibited-hours special permits (P2,000 or P4,000/yr); imported-liquor regulatory fees (wholesale/retail/amusement); distance restrictions (50m/200m from schools, churches, hospitals, public buildings); tobacco special permit (wholesale P200 / retail P50) | 3T.01–.05, 3U.01–.02 |
| **Flammables storage kinds and quantities** | Storage permit fee brackets: fuels in liters (500–2,000 up to over 100,000); film/celluloid flat; calcium carbide in cases; tar/resin in kilos; coal in tons; catch-all | 3O.01 |
| **Construction cost + occupancy group** (occupancy permit) | Fixed cost of construction per sqm by Group (Table II.G.1: P10,000/P8,000/P6,000); certificate of occupancy fee brackets by cost of construction (Divisions A, B/E/F/G/H/I, C/D) or floor area (Division J); height surcharge above 8m; change-of-use fee | 3V.01 items 1/2/8.b/10 |
| **Stall count** (markets) | Privately-owned market permit-fee brackets (first 10 stalls up to 30+); fish-broker market brackets; garbage fee per stall (public/private markets); vending-site lease/transfer/ID fees | 3A.03 items 18/63, 4F.01 Schedule J, 3Y.03 |
| Supporting fields | Pumps count (gasoline stations, 3A.03 item V); machines/lanes/tables/contrivances count (3A.03 item 44/57, 4F.01 Schedule B); horsepower of engines/machines (3N.01); kVA connected load, tons refrigeration, kW boilers, elevators, fixtures (3V.01 items 4–7, 11); cold-storage capacity cu.m. (3AB.01); firing-range count (3AA.01); heavy equipment rented out, by type (3M.01); branch count + principal-office location in/out of Malabon (3A.03, 2M.01 situs, 4F.01 Schedule S(1)(b)); business location vs. zoning (3.D.01, 3A.05(b) permit-denial grounds); community tax certificate number/date (2N.04, 3A.05(b)(1)(k), 7.07) | as cited |

***

# Appendix C — Out-of-workflow taxes documented for completeness

These impositions are transcribed in the body (Parts 1 and 11) because they sit adjacent to permit-relevant articles, but they are **not** computed in BizTrack's permit-payment flow.

| Tax | Rate (as extracted) | Why outside the permit-payment flow |
|---|---|---|
| **Professional tax** (Ch. II art. G) | P300.00/year per professional who passed the bar or a PRC board examination; payable on or before 31 January to the city where the principal office is maintained; government-employed professionals exempt | Levied on the individual professional, not on the business establishment; paid directly by the professional (annual, city of principal office) and merely *evidenced* to employers. The business-permit flow only touches it as a compliance check (employers must require proof; annual certified list of professionals due to the City Treasurer by end of March). |
| **Amusement tax on admission** (Ch. II art. H) | 10% of gross receipts from admission fees (both general and locally-produced films, per RA 9640); withheld by proprietors/lessees/operators and remitted monthly (within first 20 days of the following month); operas/concerts/dramas etc. exempt except pop/rock concerts | A recurring monthly tax return on ticket sales with its own ticket-registration and verification machinery — an ongoing tax-remittance obligation, not a fee assessed at permit application or renewal. The operator's *permit* fees are assessed under Ch. III art. A (e.g., movie houses item 43). |
| **Community tax — individual** (Ch. VII) | Basic P5.00/year + P1.00 per P1,000 of income, capped at P5,000.00; liability from age 18 with employment/business/property ≥ P1,000 assessed value; 24% p.a. interest on delinquency; exempt persons may obtain a certificate for P1.00 | Personal tax on residents, collected by the City Treasurer or deputized Barangay Treasurers, independent of any business. It intersects the permit workflow only as a documentary requirement: the Community Tax Certificate (cedula) must be exhibited when receiving any license/permit or paying any tax/fee (Sec. 7.07; new-business requirement 3A.05(b)(1)(k)) — current-year CTC, except 1 January–15 April when the preceding year's suffices. (The **corporate** community tax, Sec. 7.03 — P500 basic + P2 per P5,000 of realty value and of gross receipts, capped at P10,000 — is likewise paid outside the permit cashier flow but is a standard pre-requisite for corporate applicants.) |
